<!-- audit:ignore-start -->
**2026-09-01 correction (§243).** The table above previously read *289 unique / 20 Open
/ 269 Fixed / 154 with a write-up / 89 mention-only*. Every figure was wrong, and the
last two did not even sum to their own total (154 + 89 = 243, not 289). Two sessions
appending rows and hand-incrementing a counter is how a summary drifts by 154; I
incremented it myself twice without checking it. The counter is now derived, not typed.

**All referenced IDs now have an index row.** Sixteen did not — `e2e-bug.117`, `.119`,
and the contiguous block `.451`-`.465` (except `.455`) — and were backfilled in §300;
see *Backfilled index rows* below. Two of them had been carrying the wrong status the
whole time: `.451` and `.460` were **Open** and both were long since fixed.
**Every ID referenced in `TODO.md` now has a row here.** Seven had none — `e2e-bug.30`,
`.34`, `.36`, `.50`, `.56`, `.104`, `.121`, all pre-dating this file — and were backfilled
in §301 with each guard verified present in the tree, not merely read from the prose.

### Status labels actually in use

The rules below describe **Open** and **Fixed**, but the file has grown a wider
vocabulary and the summary now counts it honestly rather than pretending otherwise.
`Fixed` 367 · `Open` 22 · `Closed` 18 · `Partial` 5 · `Superseded` 3 · `Triaged` 2 ·
`Measured` 2 · and one each of `Mitigated`, `Withdrawn`, `Labelled`, `Re-measured`,
`Re-scoped`, `Audited`, `Mechanism`, `Mostly`.

Most of these mean *resolved, but not by fixing the reported thing* — a premise
disproved (`Withdrawn`), a ticket overtaken (`Superseded`), a measurement that replaced
a guess (`Measured`). That distinction is worth keeping; collapsing them all into
**Fixed** would lose it. **Open is the only number to plan against, and it is 22.**
<!-- audit:ignore-end -->

### Classification rules

1. Dedicated `##` section with **Fix direction** → **Open**
2. Dedicated `##` section with completed Fix/Verification → **Fixed**
3. Prose/checklist clearly says Fixed / RESOLVED → **Fixed**
4. Otherwise → actually read the mention(s) in TODO.md before tagging. Do NOT default to Open — a bug with no dedicated `##` header very often still has an inline **Fixed:** write-up plus a later "re-verification ... confirmed genuinely fixed" pass; only tag Open if the prose itself says so (no fix language, or explicit "not fixed here" / "filed, not investigated" / "still open").

_(mention-only)_ = referenced in TODO.md but no `## e2e-bug.N` heading — says nothing about fixed/open status by itself, just that there's no dedicated header. Always read the actual mention before tagging.

**2026-08-03 correction**: rule 4 previously read "Otherwise → Open (conservative; includes unclear mention-only IDs)" — this silently defaulted every mention-only bug to Open without ever checking its actual prose for a **Fixed:** write-up. Audited all 51 mention-only bugs this had mistagged (e2e-bug.101–163, .166, .167, .172, .174, .178, .186, .205, .206); every single one was already genuinely Fixed in TODO.md (root cause + fix + a later "Conclusion: ... genuinely fixed" re-verification pass) and had been Open in this file for no reason other than the flawed default. All 51 corrected to Fixed here; rule 4 rewritten above so this doesn't recur.

- [~] `e2e-bug.349` **[Partial 2026-08-08]** — **root cause 1 was already closed by §100** and nobody noticed: `isIntentAllowed('dashboard', *, 'add_services_to_cart')` is now false for every tier, because the gate reads `CommandSpec.tiers` and that command declares customer/public only. Pinned with a test. Root cause 2 fixed: `isBulkCreateCatalogPrompt` matched `category` but not `categories`, so the reported prompt registered no catalog context at all — four cues now accept the plural. **Still open:** sentence-level splitting on `.` and the `Under <Category> add <lines>` segment classifier, which the ticket itself says must be added narrowly. See AI-ROADMAP §113

- [x] `e2e-bug.351` **[Fixed 2026-08-08]** — **red means red.** `npm run test:ai-known-failures` now exits 0 over the full 1,333-suite sweep: 103 failing suites / 458 failing tests, **all quarantined**, manifest pruned 463 → 440 by the gate's own "entries that now pass must be removed" rule. §103's "7 unquarantined" was a measurement bug of mine — `json.dumps` escapes the `→`/`↔` in test names, so quarantined tests scored as unquarantined; the true count was 0 plus the one §100 regression. Five tests genuinely fixed (four stale synonym assertions superseded by e2e-bug.320, one deleted in §102). Gate deliberately kept out of the fast chain: 529s vs ~60s. See AI-ROADMAP §104

- [x] `e2e-bug.353` **[Fixed 2026-08-08]** — resolved by **renaming, not deleting**: checking first showed all six implementations live, and four files import *both* copies of the gift-card pair while aliasing one. Renamed to `isEndOfDaySummaryPushPrompt`, `isSetRetailSalesLinesFinancePrompt` and `isGiftCardCheckApplyBookCompoundPrompt` (promoting the alias already used at every call site). Inventory `duplicateSymbols` **6 → 0** and the boundary ratchet is pinned at 0. - [x] `e2e-bug.354` **[Fixed 2026-08-08]** — all thirteen deleted after triaging each against the trace corpus: the two actions with real traffic (`recommend_specialists` 198, `pay_at_venue_fallback` 12) are routed with **0 via rescue**, so the detectors added nothing; three had no traffic; eight mapped to no action at all. **First fall in the freeze ratchet**: detectors 785 → 772, baseline 790 → 777, `legacy_paraphrase` 555 → 545, ready 318 → 315. The red-means-red gate caught two stale assertions the fast chain missed. See AI-ROADMAP §107
- [ ] `e2e-bug.366` **[Open]** — `scripts/ai-completion-floor-gate.mjs` (§45) enforces the north-star completion floors and is not attached to any schedule, so nothing runs it. It is the exact failure §45 was written to fix — the metric was already measurable via §28's views and went unwatched because watching it depended on someone remembering. Needs a cadence, a runner with database access, and an alert destination; `--update` needs a separate, less frequent cadence so floors ratchet on sustained gains rather than on one good day — [`TODO.md` e2e-bug.366](./TODO.md#e2e-bug366--the-completion-floor-gate-is-not-scheduled-so-nothing-runs-it--open)
- [~] `e2e-bug.367` **[Partial 2026-08-08]** — the **5 byte-identical** `resolveServiceByName` copies (verified by hashing normalised bodies) are collapsed into `matchServiceByNameLegacy`; re-parser manifest **14 → 11**. This is a pure refactor, **not** the migration: the shared function keeps the silent-pick behaviour, and its doc says so. What changed is that adopting `EntityResolutionService.resolveService` for those handlers is now **one** clarify path to wire instead of five. The two genuine variants are left alone — merging them would be a behaviour change disguised as a refactor. See AI-ROADMAP §112
- [ ] `e2e-bug.368` **[Open, rescoped 2026-08-07]** — ~~the saga engine has no handler wiring~~ **Orchestration wired in §55**: `executePlan` now captures pre-state before each step and runs the saga on a partial failure, through injected `captureState` / `runCompensation` seams. Remaining scope is narrower and genuinely handler work: (1) concrete capture readers per command — nothing yet reads `originalStart` for `appointment.reschedule`, so inverse compensations still report `missing_capture` in production; (2) a transaction boundary per `TransactionGroup`, so same-aggregate failures roll back without compensating at all. The 6 of 14 specs declaring `none`/`manual` stay stranded by design — [`TODO.md` e2e-bug.368](./TODO.md#e2e-bug368--the-saga-engine-has-no-handler-wiring-so-nothing-is-actually-compensated--open)
- [ ] `e2e-bug.369` **[Open]** — §48's `invalidateStaleBindings` is not wired into the gateway: it needs `pendingCreatedAt` and `turnsElapsed`, and §38's round-trip envelope carries neither, so pending clarifications still have no expiry at all. Until then a stale binding can survive indefinitely and a mutating command can land on a row the user never named. - [~] `e2e-bug.371` **[Mitigated 2026-08-10 — purge outstanding]** — `AiEntityMemoryService` keys one alias map per **business** with no user dimension, and the stored shape carries `customerName`, so a customer named in one person's conversation reached every other user of that business. **Two paths, the second worse than the ticket described:** `formatEntityMemoryContextBlock` printed `customer=<name>` into the next person's classifier context (disclosure), and `applyEntityMemoryToParams` wrote `customerName` **and** `waitlistCustomerName` into **command params** — one person's customer *acted on* in another's request. **Live exposure:** 211 stored aliases across 4 businesses, **16 carrying a customerName**. Closed by `stripSharedEntityMemoryPii` on write *and* on read — read-filtering neutralises the 16 already stored without a migration. Two tests were pinning the vulnerability (one asserted `waitlistCustomerName` gets filled from a `customerName` alias) and now guard against it. **Outstanding:** purge the 16 stored names (a data decision, not done silently), and per-user memory if the capability is wanted — that needs `e2e-bug.401`. See AI-ROADMAP §156
- [x] `e2e-bug.372` **[Done 2026-08-07 — §73]** — embeddings wired: 696 vectors committed at 256 dims, `embedText` takes `dimensions`, planner calls `narrowShortlist`. Originally filed as an accuracy improvement. — §52's few-shot retriever is not wired into the planner prompt. It also needs an embedding for the incoming prompt, which is a live model call on the hot path, so wiring it has a latency and cost decision attached that the pure util does not. Separately worth recording: **do not** switch the pool to the trace table — only 68.2% of executed traces belong to intents the eval scores >=90%, 24.8% are in intents the eval never covers, and `compound_intent` has 139 executed rows at 0% accuracy — [`TODO.md` e2e-bug.372](./TODO.md#e2e-bug372--the-few-shot-retriever-is-not-wired-into-the-planner-prompt--open)
- [ ] `e2e-bug.401` **[Open]** — **nothing carries conversation state between turns**, which is the single prerequisite behind `e2e-bug.369`, `370`, `373` and `374`. The five Phase 6 modules (`ai-entity-store`, `ai-anaphora`, `ai-profile-facts`, `ai-slot-filling`, `ai-topic-change`) form a closed island: every import edge points inward and none is referenced by the gateway or pipeline. **The identity is not the blocker** — `ai-conversation.util.ts` already derives a stable `conversationId` and `turnIndex` server-side from userId/businessId/history, needs nothing from the client, and is already used as `session_id` on the trace. What is missing is persistence of an `EntityStore` keyed by that id, plus load-before/save-after around resolution. Needs a storage decision (table vs in-process cache) that depends on whether the platform runs multi-instance. See AI-ROADMAP §108

- [x] `e2e-bug.402` **[Fixed 2026-08-08]** — narrowing is now decided **per prompt** by the top retrieval score (`NARROW_MIN_TOP_SCORE = 0.5`), where truth-in-top-15 is **100%** on 197 real prompts versus 48% below 0.4. - [x] `e2e-bug.408` **[Fixed 2026-08-09]** — **`ai-command-shortlist.util.ts` was a binary file to `grep`.** §96's `commandMatchTextHash` used `\x00` and `\x01` as field delimiters and they were written into the source as literal control bytes rather than escape sequences. One NUL byte makes `grep` classify the file as binary and skip it silently — no error, no warning, no matches — in a codebase where grep is how anything gets found. It misled me twice in one session while searching for a constant declared on line 47. Replaced with `\u0000` / `\u0001`; string values unchanged, confirmed by the embedding staleness gate still matching its stored hash. See AI-ROADMAP §131
- [ ] `e2e-bug.409` **[Open]** — `ai-planner-shadow-replay.spec.ts` duplicates the replay mechanics now shared in `ai-planner-replay.util.ts` (embed, `narrowShortlist`, `buildPlannerMessages`, `decodePlanResponse`, `validatePlan`), so the two can still drift on the things that silently change a number. Not migrated with `e2e-bug.407` on purpose: it is opt-in, **no gate covers it**, and rewriting working code that nothing would catch breaking is not a safe side effect. Wants its own change, with a smoke run before and after. - [x] `e2e-bug.410` **[Fixed 2026-08-09]** — **`claim_referral_code`: 96 attempts, 0 successes.** The user types "I want to claim referral code FRIEND25"; the classifier names the action correctly every time (`action_changed_by = (none)` on all 96); `params` arrives empty; the handler replies "What referral code would you like to claim?". The extractor `enrichClaimReferralCodeParamsFromPrompt` already existed and was already tested, but was called **only from the rescue path**, and `rescueClaimReferralCodeIntent` returns null when the action is already right — so enrichment ran only when the classifier had been wrong, and it never was. Getting the action right is what broke it. `handleClaimReferralCodeLogic` now takes the prompt (as 11 sibling handlers in the same file do) and enriches from it: **96 of 96** stored prompts yield a code. The spec also declared `variables: {}`, so the planner could not fill it either — now declared, optional. **Evidence corrected in §143:** the "empty params" and "action_changed_by = none" facts are unreadable on the customer surface; the fix stands on code reading. See AI-ROADMAP §133, §143
- [x] `e2e-bug.411` **[Fixed 2026-08-09]** — **the trace outcome classifier counted questions as failures.** `resolveCommandTraceOutcome` tested `details.needsClarification` alone; handlers use `details.clarify` — **646 occurrences in 178 files** versus **19**. So a handler asking the user a question was recorded as having failed, about 34 call sites out of 35. The canonical predicate `isClarifyResult` already existed and its doc comment names the caller it was written for: "the guide fallback and **the trace writer**" — the trace writer was the one re-deriving it. Also found `ai-command-trace.util.spec.ts` was in no gate; added. See AI-ROADMAP §134
- [x] `e2e-bug.412` **[Fixed 2026-08-09]** — **the customer surface fed nothing into `ai_command_trace_step`.** `compound_intent` is the customer surface's biggest failure bucket (222 of 338), and the per-step table built to answer *which sub-intent breaks compounds* is written from `details._compoundSteps`, attached by `attachCompoundStepAttribution` — called from **`ai-command.service.ts` only**. `executeCustomerCompoundFromSteps` never called it, so this surface would have produced zero step rows when traffic resumed. Could not just be attached: with no planner there are no plan step ids, and empty ids mean `not_planned`, which would have filled `ai_command_compound_silent_drop` with compounds that dropped nothing. - [x] `e2e-bug.413` **[Fixed 2026-08-09]** — **stage attribution is blind on most of the platform.** `pipeline_trace` is present on **1,006 of 1,161** dashboard traces (87%) but only **262 of 3,703** customer (7%) and **3 of 498** provider (0.6%). `attributeActionChange` needs a `classify` entry in that trace to compare against, so `action_changed_by` and `classified_action` are null **by construction** for 93% of customer and 99% of provider traffic — regardless of what actually decided the action. Every stage-attribution analysis (which stage chose the action, which detector stole it, how often rescue helps) is therefore blind on the surface carrying **69% of traffic**, including §132's steal analysis and §133's ranking. It is now a thin wrapper around the former body, with a **per-call** sink carrying the trace back out — per-call rather than an instance field, since a shared one would leak traces between concurrent requests. Filled immediately after `understand` returns, before the blocked/clarify exits. Wrapping rather than stamping 40 returns, because the next return added would silently go untraced. **Does not fix history:** the 3,441 untraced customer rows stay unattributable, so §132 and §133 keep their caveat. See AI-ROADMAP §136, §137

- [x] `e2e-bug.417` **[Measured 2026-08-10]** — §140's 87.8% was contaminated; measured how much. Three arms over the same 343 examples: **as shipped 87.8%**, **example held out (retrieval on) 61.8%**, **example held out + retrieval off 54.2%**. Showing the model the command's own phrasing is worth **26 points**; removing retrieval costs a further 7.6 with `empty_plan` tripling to 91 — the signature of a 388-command prompt, not of the holdout. **Use 61.8%**: shipped configuration minus the contamination that mattered, still mildly optimistic because the embedding index is built from `description + examples` and still contains the held-out string (residual, needs 343 re-embeddings). Detectors reach **0%** of these by construction. Largest failure mode is `unresolved_notes` at **102 of 343**; attributing it to `e2e-bug.399`'s resolver deadlock was **withdrawn in §142** (only 3 of the 102 involve a resolver-backed required variable) and the cause is unknown. See AI-ROADMAP §141
- [ ] `e2e-bug.451` **[Open]** — **`nest build` is red on `main`, and has nothing to do with the AI registry.** `ai-propose-only.util.ts:237` fails `TS2741`: `const byReason: Record<ProposeOnlyReason, number>` is missing the `execution_unproven` key, so a member was added to `ProposeOnlyReason` without updating the one exhaustive record over it. Found in passing while verifying §163 — the file is untouched by that change and `git status` confirms it, but the point is that establishing *that* took a git check rather than a glance. A build that is already red cannot tell the next person whether they broke it, which is the same failure mode the known-failures manifest exists to prevent for tests and which the build has no equivalent of. Fix is likely one line; the question worth answering with it is whether `nest build` should join the gate chain. See AI-ROADMAP §163

- [x] `e2e-bug.452` **[Closed 2026-08-17]** — **a test rewrote two tracked files on every run.** `sync-mobile-guide-flows.spec.ts` runs the real generator, which is the point — it is what proves the committed mobile bundles still match the backend corpus — but the generator writes `syncedAt: new Date().toISOString()` into `consumer-app/src/assets/guide-flows-manifest.json` and the provider equivalent. Every sweep therefore ended with `git status` showing two modified app files nobody had touched, and the assertions never noticed because `readManifestStableFields` strips `syncedAt` before comparing: the test was asserting the right thing and littering while it did. The `SYNC_MOBILE_GUIDE_FLOWS` gate twelve lines above guards only the bootstrap path, not this run. **Fix:** capture all four files before the run and restore any that changed, in a `finally` rather than after the assertions — so a genuine failure does not also leave the tree dirty for whoever debugs it. **Verified both directions:** from a clean tree the suite now leaves 0 files modified (2/2 pass), and with a deliberately drifted manifest it still fails (1/2) while restoring the tree to exactly what it found rather than to generator output. The script itself is unchanged — it has no dry-run or output-dir option, and adding one would have been a larger change than the defect warranted. See AI-ROADMAP §179

- [ ] `e2e-bug.453` **[Open]** — **`decomposeDeterministicForSurface(surface, prompt)` takes two mutually substitutable strings.** Passing them the wrong way round is silent: it answered a question about a surface named `'List providers and check availability'` and returned `null`, which reads exactly like "this prompt does not decompose" and briefly sent §165's investigation after the wrong producer. No type, lint rule or assertion catches it, and the function has 14 call sites. Fix: take a single object (`{ surface, prompt }`), or brand `CommandSurface` so a bare string cannot be passed as one. Found in passing during §165. See AI-ROADMAP §165

- [x] `e2e-bug.454` **[Closed 2026-08-17]** — **ten pre-existing lint errors cleared, both halves verified rather than autofixed.** **Six `no-useless-escape`** in `ai-budget-service-discovery.util.ts`: every one was `\$` inside the character class `[\$€£]`, where `$` has no special meaning. The ticket said these wanted a human eye because they sit in money-parsing regexes, so equivalence was measured rather than assumed — the escaped and unescaped patterns were run over twelve money strings (`under $50`, `below $1,250.50`, `nothing over $99`, `up to dram 5000`, currency-word and bare-number forms) with **zero differences**. **Four `no-unused-vars`** in `ai-intent-rescue.service.ts`: dead imports, each asserted to occur exactly once in the file (the import itself) before removal, so a name still in use could not be stripped. `tsc` clean, both files lint-clean, roadmap gates green. **One check worth recording:** `public-booking-assistant.budget.integration` fails and is *not* in the known-failures manifest, which looked like a regression — the manifest only covers `src/modules/ai/`, and reverting the escape change reproduced the identical failure, so it is pre-existing and out of scope. Same for `ai-budget-service-discovery.util.spec.ts`, which fails to compile on a `number | undefined` argument and *is* in the manifest. See AI-ROADMAP §180

- [ ] `e2e-bug.455` **[Open]** — **the surface gate is opt-in on a copy-pasted rescue chain, so omitting it is silent.** §167 found `rescueExplainProviderAvailabilityIntent` called from two places in `ai-intent-rescue.service.ts`: the `runRescueUnknownPhase` site is wrapped in `rescueAllowedOnSurface`, the `tryRescueBusinessLanguages` site was not. `e2e-bug.435` diagnosed the exact failing prompt and fixed only the first, so the bug outlived its own ticket. The failure mode is worse than a misroute: a surface-illegal rescue is dropped by `acceptRescueForSurface`, which **blocks the correct rescue behind it** and resolves the prompt to `null` — so the symptom presents as a missing detector rather than a wrong one, and does not resemble the ticket that describes it. `rescueAllowedOnSurface`'s own doc comment already calls registry-consultation "the cheap half of the fix; the structural half is in the ticket" — this is that ticket. Fix: make the gate structural rather than per-call-site (gate once where the phase result is assembled, or make the rescue helpers take surface and refuse to compile without it) so a new call site cannot omit it. **Measured in §168: 91 of 5,927 rescue-expecting cases resolve to `null` after producing a surface-illegal intent, across 21 producers** (heaviest: `tryRescueMarketingGrowth`→`open_billing_settings` 9, `tryRescueProviderOpenShifts`→`list_waitlist_for_my_services` 9, `tryRescueAddBookingToCalendar` 5, `tryRescueMarketingGrowth`→`summarize_loyalty_program` 5). These are latent service defects, **not** 91 failing tests — the eval reaches the rescue through several specialised entry points, so most never take the generic path. **The fix is proven safe:** `acceptRescueForSurface` is applied to all three phase results in `ai-intent-rescue-pipeline.util.ts`, so any candidate `gateRescueBySurface` drops would be dropped downstream anyway — the gate can only turn a `null` into a legal rescue, never the reverse. Apply it at every phase consumption point (gate where the phase consumes a helper's result, not at the inner producer: that covers every intent the helper can return, which `tryRescueCatalog` required). §168 did four sites; 21 remain. See AI-ROADMAP §167

- [ ] `e2e-bug.456` **[Open — product decision]** — **`e2e137-professional-profile` is unsatisfiable as written.** It expects `'Show me Mariam Ohanyan's professional profile'` on **dashboard** to rescue to `explain_professional_profile`. The rescue produces that action correctly; it is then dropped because the spec puts the command on `["customer","public"]`. That is not an oversight — the whole family is consumer-facing (`explain_provider_availability` and `explain_provider_specialty` are also `["customer","public"]`, and `e2e-bug.435`'s comment states it). A search of the specs for a dashboard command covering "view a staff member's profile" returns nothing, so `e2e-bug.137`'s requirement ("out-of-toolset domains must rescue to real dashboard actions") has no target here. **Decide:** either add `dashboard` to that command's surfaces — which changes the dashboard's catalogue, shortlists and permissions — or retire the scenario. Both are product calls. Grouped with the other Wave 0 items awaiting a non-engineering answer (401 ops, 366 ops, 405 product). See AI-ROADMAP §168 **Costed 2026-08-20 — the options are no longer symmetric, and one hypothesis was wrong.** (a) *Doing nothing* is not neutral: on dashboard the prompt rescues to **`none`**, so a staff member asking for a colleague's profile gets **no answer at all**. (b) *Adding `dashboard`* would give a **good** answer, not a generic explainer — I assumed the opposite from the spec's description ("Explain what is on a provider professional profile") and checked: `buildExplainProfessionalProfileSummary` takes a `ProviderProfileSnapshot` and returns *"Opening Mariam's profile (role). Specialty: …"*, so the handler already supports a named provider. The command is **T0 with no required variables**, so the catalogue/shortlist/permission change is about *exposure*, not risk. (c) *No alternative exists*: `provider.show_profile` is `surfaces: ['provider']` and means the provider's **own** profile, so it does not cover this. Either option takes the corpus to **8,464/8,464 = 100%**. **So the question is not corpus hygiene — it is whether dashboard staff may look up a colleague's profile.** If yes, (b) is small and the handler is ready; if no, retire the scenario and the gap stays named here rather than hidden in a failing corpus row.

- [x] `e2e-bug.457` **[Closed 2026-08-17]** — **22 eval cases claimed Armenian coverage and contained none; now 22 of 22 contain Armenian.** Every `hy` row in `ai-budget-discover-and-book-compound-multilingual.fixtures.ts` and its rank twin was the English prompt verbatim, so the cases passed *because* they were English and asserted nothing about Armenian. Translated in the same style the `ru` rows use — keep the English scaffolding, translate one object — using only tokens already present elsewhere in this corpus (`վաղը`, `ով է ազատ`). **The fake coverage was load-bearing:** §169 fixed `hasCheckStepCue` for Russian and shipped no Armenian half, and nothing caught it because there was no Armenian case that could fail. Armenian cues are now in both copies of that helper. **Making the coverage real surfaced four genuine failures** — see `e2e-bug.465`. Corpus went 2 → 6, and the four new failures were **fixed rather than absorbed** — see `e2e-bug.465`, closed the same day. The baseline raise this ticket originally recorded was reverted; the corpus is back to 2 with the Armenian coverage real. See AI-ROADMAP §181

- [x] `e2e-bug.465` **[Closed 2026-08-17 — fixed, after I talked myself out of it once]** — **`isCheckProvidersForServicePrompt` rejected Armenian compounds, so four `hy` cases could not decompose.** Uncovered by `e2e-bug.457` the moment the fixtures stopped being English.
  **I filed this as deferred and that was the wrong call.** The stated reason — the predicate has eight-plus branches and negative guards delegating to siblings, so widening it is the risky pattern §173/§174 kept undoing — was about the *wrong part of the function*. Its final clause is already `isMultilingualCheckProvidersPrompt`, and `ով`/`ազատ` were already in that seam's token sets. The vocabulary and the extension point both existed.
  **What forced the re-look:** the sweep showed the blast radius was **6**, not 4 — two of them `keeps eval golden compound cases aligned with decomposition runtime` and `passes every compound scenario through eval runner`. Absorbing those into the known-failures list would have disabled guards that catch future corpus/runtime drift, which is a worse trade than the gap.
  **Root cause, found by binary search rather than by reading eleven guards:** stripping the trailing "book the earliest slot" made the Armenian form pass, naming the clause exactly. The **"timed book alone"** guard returns false for a bare timed booking *unless* the prompt also mentions who/which/anyone/providers — an escape that exists precisely so a compound is not mistaken for a bare booking, and that alternation was English-only. The identical Armenian sentence therefore fell through to `return false`.
  **Fix:** the escape now also consults `MULTILINGUAL_WHO` and `MULTILINGUAL_PROVIDER_AVAILABILITY`, composed from those existing constants rather than new inline literals so the vocabulary keeps one home; `MULTILINGUAL_WHO` is exported for it. Corpus **6 → 2**, all 143 discover-and-book tests pass, and the deliberate baseline raise recorded under `e2e-bug.457` is reverted — it is no longer warranted. See AI-ROADMAP §183

- [x] `e2e-bug.458` **[Closed 2026-08-14]** — **`ai-e2e137-routing.util.spec.ts` tested a reimplementation of the routing it is named for.** It built its own `??` cascade of the rescue predicates instead of calling `AiIntentRescueService`, so it always agreed with itself. Proven twice over: it stayed green for six weeks after the service gained the `customer_retention_rate` producer on 2026-08-02, and then went red in §172 *because* §171 corrected the fixtures to match reality — a test failing because the code was fixed is the signature of a mirror. **Fixed in §172:** the spec now constructs `AiIntentRescueService` and calls `.rescue(...)`; the five imports that existed only to feed the cascade are deleted. 12 of 13 pass; the remaining one is `professional-profile`, a genuine divergence the mirror was concealing, tracked as the product decision `e2e-bug.456`. See AI-ROADMAP §172

- [x] `e2e-bug.459` **[Closed 2026-08-17 — the finding was mine and it was wrong]** — filed as *"635 duplicate `prompt + surface` groups; 11 disagree on `rescueReason`; 0 disagree on the action"*. The **11 is wrong**, and wrong in exactly the way the ticket's own caveat warned about. That caveat recorded a first cut reporting "126 conflicting" which counted pairs where one side asserted no rescue at all — and the corrected three-tier count then made the *same* mistake one level down, treating a row that asserts no `rescueReason` as disagreeing with one that does. Re-measured 2026-08-17 counting only groups where **two or more rows each assert a non-null `rescueReason` and those differ**: **0**. Every one of the 11 was `X` versus `(none)` — one case checking less than another, which is legitimate and common (`e2e129-visits-left-plan` asserts the action only; `explain-my-subscription-visits-left-plan-customer` also pins the reason). **635 duplicate groups remain and are still worth a tidy-up someday**, but they are redundancy, not contradiction: no duplicate pair in the corpus contradicts another on either the action or the reason. Nothing to fix. See AI-ROADMAP §182

- [x] `e2e-bug.460` **[Closed 2026-08-17 (§189) — fixed by inverting the question]** — **`"Set Downtown's phone number to 555-0100"` resolves to `update_employee` with `employeeName: 'Downtown'` instead of `update_location` with `locationName: 'Downtown'`** (`e2e146-locations-update-downtown-phone`, the last actionable corpus failure). `tryRescueStaffOperations` claims it before `isUpdateLocationPrompt` runs. **The usual guard does not work here, and this was measured rather than assumed:** `isUpdateLocationPrompt` is also **true** for `"Set Maria's phone number to 555-0100"` and `"Update Gevorg's phone to 555-0199"`, which are genuine employee prompts. "Set X's phone number to N" is grammatically identical whether X is a person or a place, so no wording-based predicate can separate them and guarding the staff chain with this one would break employee phone updates. **The principled fix is data-backed:** `IntentRescueInput` already carries `employees`, so the rule is "if the captured name matches a known employee → `update_employee`, else fall through to the location rescue". Two blockers: `tryRescueStaffOperations(prompt, action)` does not receive `employees` (needs threading from `input`), and — the real question — it is not established that `input.employees` is reliably complete in production; it flows from the pipeline caller, and if it is ever partial or empty this rule would silently stop claiming legitimate employee updates. **Decide first whether `input.employees` is guaranteed complete at this point**, then thread it. Found and diagnosed in §174. See AI-ROADMAP §174
  **Blocking question answered 2026-08-17 (§188): `input.employees` IS complete.** Every production loader is `employeeRepo.find({ where: { businessId, isActive: true } })` with no `take`, `skip` or pagination — `ai-command.service.ts:575`, `provider-ai-command.service.ts:5881`, and the four suggestion/briefing/report services. When the list is supplied it is the full active roster, so the completeness worry that blocked this ticket is resolved. (One caveat: `isActive: true` excludes deactivated staff, so a prompt naming a deactivated employee would look like a stranger.)
  **But the proposed rule is unsound, and the corpus says so: 2 → 89 failures.** "If the captured name matches a known employee → `update_employee`, else fall through" breaks every staff command whose subject is *not yet* an employee — measured `create_employee` **3/33**, `invite_staff_member` **17/33**, `deactivate_employee` **8/33**. That is not an eval artefact: you name a new hire when creating or inviting them, in production too.
  **Scoping it to `update_employee` alone removes that class but is still unmeasurable here: 18 failures, 16 of them inside `update_employee`.** The cause is `e2e-bug.470` — the eval passes a fixed 3-person roster to all 8,464 cases, and 17 `update_employee` cases name people outside it. So the scoped rule is *not proven wrong*, it is *untestable* until the fixture can express a per-case roster.
  **Two ways forward, and the second is better.** (a) Fix `e2e-bug.470` first, then the `update_employee`-scoped roster rule becomes measurable. (b) Match **positively on locations** instead — "is Downtown a known location?" needs no assumption about roster completeness and cannot misfire on new hires. `IntentRescueInput` carries `employees` and `customers` but **no `locations`**, so (b) needs a new input field threaded from the same catalog load that already fetches them. Prefer (b): it answers the actual question the prompt poses, rather than inferring a location from the absence of a person.
  **Closed 2026-08-17 with (b), §189. Corpus 8,462 → 8,463 / 8,464 (99.98% → 99.99%); `update_location` is 2/2.** `tryRescueStaffOperations` now declines when the name it extracted is a **known location**, letting the chain reach the location rescue — which was already correct on this prompt (`update_location`, `locationName: "Downtown"`, `phone: "555-0100"`, verified before any wiring was written). The guard only ever gives up a claim; it never asserts another domain's intent, and it cannot touch `create_employee`/`invite_staff_member`, because a new hire's name is not a location — which is exactly what sank the inverted form at −1.03%. `locations` is threaded `BusinessCatalog` → dashboard adapter → `PipelineUnderstandInput` → `IntentRescueInput`, joining the existing `Promise.all` rather than adding a round trip. **Two things deliberately left:** the field is optional on `BusinessCatalog`, so a producer that omits it degrades to today's behaviour (measured: with the rule live but no locations supplied, the corpus stayed at exactly 2); and the provider adapter does not supply locations — **§190 confirmed that is correct, not a gap**: `locations.update` declares `surfaces: ['dashboard']`, so `update_location` is unreachable from the provider app and threading it there would attach to no behaviour. `e2e-bug.470` is no longer a blocker here — choosing the location polarity is what removed the dependency on roster completeness — but it still stands for any future rule that reads employees.

- [ ] `e2e-bug.461` **[Open]** — **`block_resource_unavailable` and `deactivate_resource` are the same command with different names.** Found while declaring T1 inputs (§177). `handleBlockResourceUnavailableLogic` resolves the resource through the same `resolveResource` (`resourceId` then `resourceName`) and then calls **the same** `deps.resourcesService.deactivateResource(businessId, resource.id)` that `handleDeactivateResourceLogic` calls. Same inputs, same effect. **Despite the name there is no time window** — no start, end, date or reason param exists — yet the success message promises one: *"marked unavailable — deactivate until re-enabled in settings"*, which describes a state the command cannot set and the user cannot undo except manually. Two possibilities and they need different fixes: either the intent is redundant and should be retired into `deactivate_resource` (an alias), or it was meant to take a window and the implementation was never written — in which case the summary is describing unbuilt behaviour, which is the "capability built, never connected" class in reverse. Declaring the inputs honestly is what made this visible: the spec now says it takes only a resource, which reads oddly next to the name. **Second instance found §177 slice 8 — this is a pattern, not a one-off:** `handleAcceptDeliveryLogic` is a one-line `return handleMarkOutForDeliveryLogic(deps, businessId, params, userId, prompt)`, so `accept_delivery` and `mark_out_for_delivery` are the same command with two names and two specs. Unlike the resource pair the names here describe genuinely different workflow steps — a courier accepting a handover is not the order going out for delivery — so if the distinction is real the implementation is missing, and if it is not, one of the two should be an alias. Worth sweeping the other `handle*Logic` one-line delegations for more of these before deciding. **Third instance, §177 slice 30:** `handleManageNotificationPreferencesLogic` is a one-line `return deps.pushNotifications.handleEnableNotifications(...)` with the action relabelled, so `manage_notification_preferences` and `enable_notifications` are the same command. This one looks deliberate rather than accidental — the relabelling is explicit — so it is an *alias* wearing a second spec, and the fix is probably to declare it as one rather than to split them. Three instances across 30 slices suggests the sweep is worth doing.
  **Sweep done 2026-08-17, and it bounds the problem rather than growing it.** Scanning every exported `handle*Logic` whose entire body is `return handleOther(...)` finds **12**, which classify cleanly:
  - **6 product-guide handlers** (`explain_app_feature`, `guide_user_flow`, `explain_current_screen`, plus async twins) each pass a **distinct intent discriminator** into `handleProductGuideIntentLogic`. Different commands sharing a renderer — legitimate, and visibly so at the call site.
  - **5 `*LogicImpl` / `*LogicCore` splits** — one command whose implementation is factored in two. Not duplication.
  - **1 true duplicate**: `handleAcceptDeliveryLogic -> handleMarkOutForDeliveryLogic`, already known, and the tell is that it forwards **the same arguments with no discriminator**.
  **So no new instances among exported functions.** That discriminator/no-discriminator distinction is the reusable test: a shared implementation reached with a distinguishing argument is design; reached with identical arguments it is a second name for one command.
  **What the sweep cannot see, stated plainly:** two of the three known duplicates (`block_resource_unavailable`, `manage_notification_preferences`) are **service methods**, not exported functions, and a grep of that shape returns only the ordinary `handleX -> handleXLogic` service→logic pattern — it cannot distinguish a cross-command forward there. Both were found by reading during slices, not by search. **Three is therefore a floor, not a confirmed total.**
  **Ready to decide.** `accept_delivery`/`mark_out_for_delivery`: names describe genuinely different workflow steps (a courier accepting a handover is not the order going out), so either the distinction is real and unimplemented, or one should be an alias. `block_resource_unavailable`/`deactivate_resource`: near-synonyms, and the former's summary promises a time window it has no parameter for — likeliest an alias. `manage_notification_preferences`/`enable_notifications`: the relabelling is explicit, so almost certainly a deliberate alias that should be declared as one. See AI-ROADMAP §177

- [ ] `e2e-bug.462` **[Open — latent]** — **`request_time_off`'s completion validator accepts an alias its handler never reads.** `provider-command-completion.validator.ts:402` passes the request as complete when `params.startDate || params.date || params.dateFrom` is set; `handleRequestTimeOffLogic` reads only `params.startDate || params.date` and returns *"Specify the dates you need off"* otherwise. So a request carrying `dateFrom` is judged complete and then refused — the user is asked for something they just supplied, which is exactly `e2e-bug.410`'s shape and C2's founding example. **Latent, not live — measured, not assumed:** `dateFrom`/`dateTo` is a real convention the classifier teaches for ranges (`list_schedule_gaps`, `day_replan`, `onboard_provider_schedule`, `create_direct_schedule`, `clear_schedule`), but every one of those rules lives in the **dashboard** classifier fixtures, and `request_time_off` is a **provider**-surface command that appears in no classifier fixture at all. Nothing currently emits `dateFrom` here. It matters because time off is inherently a range, so `dateFrom` is the natural thing for a future prompt or rule to produce. Fix: either drop `dateFrom` from the validator, or read it in the handler alongside `startDate`/`date` — the second is likelier right. Found while declaring T1 inputs (§177). See AI-ROADMAP §177

- [x] `e2e-bug.463` **[Closed 2026-08-16]** — **the reachability ratchet could not tell "handled by a switch" from "handled by nothing", and one command really was unexecutable.** The ratchet counted `undispatchable <= 291`, conflating migration debt with defects; its own comment conceded it. **Split into two assertions:** the old count stays as debt, and a new named list `NO_EXECUTOR` holds rows reachable by neither a dispatch map nor a `case`/`if` branch — named rather than counted, because a count can be satisfied by fixing a different one. **The instance was real:** `push.confirm_booking_from_push` had no executor anywhere, while `isConfirmPendingBookingPrompt` deliberately declined every push/notification prompt in its favour (its own comment says so, citing `e2e-bug.242`) and a rescue produced the action. A provider saying *"confirm that booking from the notification"* got no reply at all. Fixed by implementing `handleConfirmBookingFromPushLogic`: booking resolution mirrors `open_booking_from_push` (explicit `bookingId`, then the push payload, then the prompt) and the confirm goes through `providerMobileService.updateBooking`, the same path the provider surface's own `confirm_pending_booking` uses, so access control and side effects are not reimplemented. **Two scanner corrections worth recording, because both produced false alarms:** an `ai/`-only directory scan reported **78** commands as dead — provider commands are switch-handled in `provider-mobile/` — and a `case '…':`-only matcher reported `submit_review_with_token` as dead when it is dispatched by `if (action === '…')`. `unknown` is excluded as the classifier's fallback sentinel. `NO_EXECUTOR` is now empty and asserted so. See AI-ROADMAP §178

- [ ] `e2e-bug.464` **[Open — security; escalated 2026-08-16]** — **a live OpenAI API key is solicited in chat, scraped from the prompt by regex, and stored unredacted.** Filed from `configure_zendesk`'s `apiToken`; the sweep it asked for found a worse instance, and the whole chain is verified:
  1. **The system asks for it.** `command-completion.validator.ts:1212` returns the message *"Specify platform default or tenant OpenAI API key (sk-…)"* — a clarify prompt inviting the user to type a secret into the conversation.
  2. **It is scraped from free text.** `parseApiKey` in `ai-openai-integration.util.ts:182` reads `params.apiKey` and, failing that, matches the prompt against `/\b(sk-[A-Za-z0-9_-]{8,})\b/` — deliberate extraction of an `sk-` key from natural language, owned by `integration.configure_openai` / `configure_openai_integration`.
  3. **It is persisted in the clear.** `buildAiCommandTraceRow` (`ai-command-trace.util.ts:133`) sets `promptNormalized = input.promptNormalized ?? input.promptRaw` and stores `promptRaw`; a grep for redaction/sanitisation over the AI module finds `sanitizeParamsForPreview` (confirmation copy, `e2e-bug.156`) and `sanitizeListServicesFilterValue` — **nothing that strips secrets before storage**, and no handling of `sk-` anywhere outside that validator message.
  **Credential-shaped params across the AI module** (`params.*`): `apiKey` ×7, `token` ×7, `manageToken` ×6, `apiKeyName` ×3, `idToken` ×1, `apiToken` ×1. The distinction that matters is *presented* vs *set*: `manageToken`/`token` are guest-booking links the user already holds and legitimately pastes, and `apiKeyName` is a label. The ones being **set** are `apiKey` (OpenAI) and `apiToken` (Zendesk).
  **The three open questions, verified 2026-08-16 — all three answers make it worse, not better:**
  - **Is a trace row actually written?** **Yes, unconditionally.** `ai-gateway.service.ts:543` reads `if (!shouldPersistCommandTrace(opts.result)) return;` then calls `commandTrace.recordFireAndForget(input)`. `shouldPersistCommandTrace` (`ai-command-trace-recorder.util.ts:230`) returns `false` only when `result.details.tracePersisted === true` — a **de-duplication guard, not a filter**. There is no action-based exclusion, so `configure_openai_integration` persists like every other command. `ai-command-trace.service.ts:40` is the single save site (`traceRepo.save(traceRepo.create(row))`).
  - **Retention?** **None.** No `DELETE` against the table anywhere in `src` (grepped `traceRepo.delete`/`.remove`/`delete … ai_command_trace`), no TTL or expiry column on `AiCommandTrace`, and `prompt_raw` is a **non-nullable `text`** column. Rows persist indefinitely.
  - **Anything outside the table?** **Logs: no. The LLM: yes.** No logger call in the AI module interpolates prompt text — `ai-prompt-security.service.ts` logs signals and block reasons only, never the prompt. But `ai-command-plan.prompt.ts:215` pushes `{ role: 'user', content: message }`, so the raw prompt is transmitted to OpenAI as message content on the planning/classification path. A pasted `sk-` key therefore leaves the system in the request body as well as landing in the database.
  **Net:** a key typed at the validator's own invitation is sent to a third party and then stored forever, unredacted. **Still unverified (ops, not code):** whether this feature is enabled in production and whether any real key has actually been pasted — that decides incident-vs-defect, and it is a question for whoever owns the deployment, not something the repository can answer. Fix direction: stop soliciting the key conversationally (settings form), and add prompt-level redaction at the trace boundary regardless — a `sk-` pattern in `promptRaw` should never be stored. Found in §177 slice 11's sweep. See AI-ROADMAP §177

- [ ] `e2e-bug.418` **[Open]** — **6% of the command registry declares its inputs.** **41 of 696** specs declare any variables; **655 declare none**, and **297 of those are mutating** (T1 163, T2 109, T3 25) — including `payment.adjust_gift_card_balance`, a T2 money command whose spec says it takes nothing. `appointment.reschedule` is one of the 41 and fully declared, which is why `e2e-bug.399` was written about it: the one command examined closely is unrepresentative. This is the general case of `e2e-bug.410` — `claim_referral_code` declared `variables: {}` while its handler read `params.referralCode`, and 96 users were asked for a code they had just typed; 654 commands share that shape. `e2e-bug.379` counted the port complete at 696/696 by spec, which is 41/696 by declared inputs, while `CommandSpec` is described throughout as the single source of truth for what a command takes. Start with the **134 T2/T3** specs — money and bulk. See AI-ROADMAP §142 **Verified closed 2026-08-21, all four tiers.** T1 163 → 0 (§178), T0 367 → 0 (§190→§214→§215); every ceiling is now a per-command conformance rule. Re-ran it today: `ai-declared-inputs.rule.spec.ts` **56 tests green**, with `T0_UNDECLARED` asserting `[]`. Note the raw census still shows 98 T0 specs with empty `variables` — those are the named `VERIFIED_NO_INPUT` exemptions, not a backlog. tech-debt §C2. |
- [x] `e2e-bug.419` **[Fixed 2026-08-10]** — **the customer surface never recorded its params.** `extractCommandTraceMetadata` reads them from `details.partialParams ?? enrichedParams ?? previewParams ?? params`; the customer service writes none, so **99.4%** of its 3,703 traces store `{}` against the dashboard's 83.5%. Third instance of the same shape after `failure_reason` (§134) and `pipeline_trace` (§136/§137), all one root cause: the customer path was never wired into the telemetry the dashboard has had since §1.1. **Costs `e2e-bug.410` two of its three stated facts** — "params arrived empty" and "`action_changed_by` = none" are both unreadable on this surface; the fix still stands on code reading (`enrichClaimReferralCodeParamsFromPrompt` is called only from the rescue path, which returns null when the action is already right). Fixed by having `stampCommandTraceDetails` write `params`, supplied through §137's per-call sink; `?? result.details?.params` keeps the dashboard byte-identical. See AI-ROADMAP §143
- [x] `e2e-bug.420` **[Fixed 2026-08-10]** — **the provider surface had the same telemetry gaps as the customer one.** `pipelineTrace` appeared **once** in `provider-ai-command.service.ts`, in the `blocked` branch, out of 233 returns: **3 of 498** provider traces carry a pipeline trace (0.6%) and **98.2%** store empty params, so no provider action's deciding stage or inputs were ever recorded. Fixed with §137's pattern — `executeCommand` wraps the former body and a per-call sink carries trace, confidence and params, filled before the blocked/clarify exits. Renaming the body moved three detectors in the checked-in inventory and `ai-command-inventory.boundary` failed correctly; regenerated via `npm run build:ai-inventory`. See AI-ROADMAP §144
- [x] `e2e-bug.421` **[Fixed 2026-08-10]** — `provider-mobile` was outside the known-failures sweep with 11 unreported failures. Triaged rather than imported: **10 were tests rotting against the calendar** — fixtures naming `2026-06-20` against utils that reject dates more than a day past `Date.now()`, and a `win_back` badge that appeared once the fixture's `2026-05-01` visit crossed the 90-day inactivity threshold. Fixed by freezing the clock (`jest.useFakeTimers({ now })`) rather than moving to relative dates, which would make the fixtures a second implementation of the code under test. **The 11th was a stub that outlived its refactor**: the harness passed a literal `'Harness provider classifier context'` to `deps.classify`, so a test asserting the classifier prompt carries the intelligence blocks was asserting on the stub — production builds them correctly via `buildProviderClassifierAppendix`. Sweep pattern now `modules/(ai|provider-mobile)/`: **1,339 -> 1,432 suites**, **33,655 -> 34,456 tests**, failures unchanged at 102/457, manifest exact. See AI-ROADMAP §145
- [x] `e2e-bug.422` **[Fixed 2026-08-10]** — a lint cannot find calendar rot: **340** files carry an ISO date literal and only **8** freeze the clock, so flagging them all would report 332 non-problems. Built a detector instead — `test/time-travel.setup.cjs` shifts what the process believes *now* is (`TIME_TRAVEL_DAYS=400 npm run test:ai-known-failures`), moving only zero-argument `new Date()` and `Date.now()` so fixture literals keep their meaning, and shifting `Date` rather than faking timers so failures are about dates and not about `setTimeout`. Inert without the flag. Found **10** tests the manifest does not list. See AI-ROADMAP §146
- [x] `e2e-bug.423` **[Fixed 2026-08-10]** — all nine date-dependent tests fixed by freezing the clock (`Date` only; timers stay real). `ai-list-my-upcoming-appointments.logic`, `ai-self-service-booking.logic`, `ai-e2e289-tour-calendar-week-locale`, `ai-booking-depth.util`, `provider-mobile-time-off`, `provider-mobile-self-block`. **`ai-e2e289` needed a second attempt:** frozen to 2026-08-01 it failed immediately, because the fixture's week starts 2026-08-03 and from the 1st that is *next* week — a freeze date must land inside the window the fixture describes, not merely before it. Caught because three of the four failures were known manifest entries and the fourth was not. **`TIME_TRAVEL_DAYS=400` now matches the manifest exactly** (102 suites / 457 tests), identical to a normal run: the suite is indifferent to the date across 1,432 suites and 34,457 tests. Does not prevent new rot — the detector finds it, nothing stops it. See AI-ROADMAP §147
- [x] `e2e-bug.424` **[Fixed 2026-08-10]** — **nine provider `explain_*` commands scored 0% across 241 eval cases because a surface gate discarded their correct rescues.** Detectors fire (`isExplainTodayTimelinePrompt` → true), are wired at two call sites, and `runRescueUnknownPhase` returns the right action when called directly — but the full pipeline returns `none`, because `acceptRescueForSurface` asks `isIntentAllowedOnSurface`, which reads `COMMAND_REGISTRY`, where none of the nine had an entry (and no `CommandSpec` either). `e2e-bug.379`'s parallel lists at their most expensive: a command with a detector, a handler and eval coverage, invisible to the list that decides whether its surface may use it. Fixed by adding nine ids to `PROVIDER_EXCLUSIVE_INTENTS`: **398 → 147** deterministic failures, corpus **95.30% → 98.26%**, no regressed intents, manifest **445 → 286**. Phase 8 freeze 544 → 548 and reachability 282 → 291, both recorded with reasons; all nine verified to have real handlers before registering. See AI-ROADMAP §149
- [x] `e2e-bug.425` **[Fixed 2026-08-10]** — **`compound_intent` was discarded on every surface.** `acceptRescueForSurface` asks `isIntentAllowedOnSurface`, which answers `false` because `compound_intent` is not in `COMMAND_REGISTRY` and should not be — it is a routing outcome ('this message contains several requests'), and its *steps* are the commands, each surface-checked at dispatch. The decomposer worked and the gate threw the result away: **48 of 48** eval cases failed with `got none`. Fixed by listing it beside `unknown` / `error` / `security_blocked`, the pseudo-actions already exempted for the same reason; registering it as a command would have put it into command lists, dispatch reachability and the spec inventory. **147 → 99** failures, **98.26% → 98.83%**, manifest **286 → 273**. See AI-ROADMAP §150

Booking-depth util suite 53/53. tech-debt §D4. |
- [x] `e2e-bug.427` **[Fixed 2026-08-10]** — **a routing fix nearly promoted `compound_intent` to autonomous execution.** `e2e-bug.425` took its eval accuracy 0% → 100%, and `resolveExecutionMode` promotes on accuracy — so a multi-step command would have moved from `propose_only` to `autonomous` with nothing about its *execution* having changed. The corpus asserts `rescuedAction`, which for a compound says only that the message was recognised as multi-step; §135 measured compound failures dominated by one constituent step (`book_nearest_slot`, 148 of 222), which no `rescuedAction` assertion can see. - [x] `e2e-bug.428` **[Fixed 2026-08-10]** — **two category extractors skipped their own guards.** `extractBudgetDiscoverServiceCategory` and `extractRankDiscoverServiceCategory` both start with `enrichListServicesParamsFromPrompt` and returned its answer directly, bypassing the two checks every other path in the same function runs: `SERVICE_CATEGORY_BLOCKLIST` (which already lists `"affordable"`, taken as the category in "affordable styling options") and the singulariser (so `"facials"` never became `"facial"`). Seven `compoundStepParams[0].params.serviceCategory` mismatches, one shape, two files — no others, checked. **99 → 93** failures, **98.83% → 98.90%**, the affected compound recipe **89.74% → 97.44%**, manifest **273 → 271**, no regressions. - [x] `e2e-bug.429` **[Fixed 2026-08-10]** — **`diagnose_tour_capacity` treated the word "checkout" as a tour topic**, taking **8 of 9** `fix_checkout_validation_error` failures on prompts with no tour in them ("Checkout won't accept my email even though it's there"). `isDiagnoseTourCapacityPrompt` requires a checkout-rejection cue *and* a tour topic, but the topic test listed `checkout|booking page`, so the two conditions collapsed into one. **Four attempts, each scored on the 8,464-case corpus:** removing `checkout` from the topic cost 5 genuine tour cases; adding tour vocabulary cost 2 more elsewhere; the word `tour` alone still cost 2. The clean fix is a **negative** guard — a checkout complaint naming a *form field* (email, phone, privacy checkbox, contact fields) is not a capacity problem. Positive vocabulary could never have worked: one kept case names its tour only by name ("Mountain Trek"). **93 → 86** failures, **98.90% → 98.98%**, `fix_checkout_validation_error` 68.97% → **93.1%**, tour capacity back to **100%**, no regressions. See AI-ROADMAP §152
- [x] `e2e-bug.430` **[Fixed 2026-08-10]** — **`deactivate_service` 50% → 100%**, two unrelated faults. (2) "Disable Deluxe Facial from the service catalog" captured `serviceName: "Deluxe Facial from the"` — the pattern anchors on `\s+service\b` and the word appears twice, so the greedy capture ran to the second. **86 → 78** failures, **98.98% → 99.08%**, no regressions. **Note:** an identical pair of fixes in `ai-catalog.util.ts` measured **no change** (a producer in `ai-intent-rescue.service.ts` fires first); kept because the defect is the same and the path is live, but recorded as unverified. - [ ] `e2e-bug.435` **[Open]** — the intent-rescue chain is **first-match-wins with no surface awareness**: a rescue whose action is illegal on the current surface can claim a prompt, be discarded by `acceptRescueForSurface`, and leave the user with **nothing** — while the correct surface-appropriate rescue sits later in the same chain, never consulted. Found via `summarize_my_appointments` (4 eval cases, provider surface): a dashboard-only `summarize_bookings` rescue at `ai-intent-rescue.service.ts:974` shadowed the working provider rescue at :1594, in *both* the classified and unknown phases. - [x] `e2e-bug.433` **[Fixed 2026-08-10]** — **`explain_provider_availability` read "dates displayed" as a provider's name**, and was stealing **17 of the 69** remaining eval failures across eight victim intents. `isExplainProviderAvailabilityPrompt` treats a successfully extracted name as proof of a named-schedule question, so every guard beneath it (booking verbs, soonest-slot, ranking, specialty) is bypassed — and `PROVIDER_NAME_BLOCKLIST` is exact-match on **single words**, so "VAT included", "amounts shown", "the expert" and "GST" all passed. Added `looksLikeProviderName`: no blocklisted token anywhere, at most three tokens, and one token reading as a proper noun (initial capital, not ALL-CAPS) — with an explicit exception for an all-lower-case prompt, where capitalisation carries no signal and one token is as much as can be inferred. **69 → 51** failures, **99.18% → 99.40%**, **ten intents improved, none regressed**, manifest **262 → 246**. Two of the 17 remain: "Same time with Maria instead" extracts the name correctly and belongs to `switch_provider_same_time` — a sibling exclusion, left separate to stay attributable. - [x] `e2e-bug.434` **[Fixed 2026-08-14]** — **`recommend_specialists` took "Who is best for curly hair?"** from `explain_provider_specialty` — 2 of 36, the same prompt on two surfaces, so one cause. Excluding specialty reads from `isProviderRankDiscoveryPrompt` therefore costs ranking nothing it uniquely owns; the guard sits above `isServiceCatalogRankSpecialistPrompt`, whose bare `return true` would otherwise short-circuit it (§157's shape). **27 → 25** failures, **99.68% → 99.70%**, no regressions. - [~] `e2e-bug.405` **[Measured 2026-08-08]** — the runtime model is the one running: commands **in** the confirm list show 24 approvals for 23 executions; commands **absent** from it show **211 executions and 0 approvals**. But most of the gap is not a defect — the T2 bulk is customer-surface payment (`pay_online`, `apply_gift_card_code`), where the checkout **is** the confirmation and the specs over-declare via the blanket `ALWAYS_CONFIRM_TIERS` rule. The real residue is **13 commands / 36 traces executed on the dashboard**, led by `operations.optimize_schedule` (**T3**, 13x) and `commerce.record_expense` (T2, 6x). Not auto-added to the gate: which operations warrant interrupting a user is a product decision, and the list is short enough to review. See AI-ROADMAP §122
- [x] `e2e-bug.404` **[Fixed 2026-08-09]** — the routing seam declined every plan needing confirmation (`decidePlannerRoute` condition 4), `reject:needs_confirmation` on **24 of 121** rescue-dependent prompts — the binding constraint on the planner's mutating half. Fixed by building the handshake rather than relaxing the check: `shouldConfirmBeforeExecute` re-derives the spec requirement at the execute gate, closing the 151-command gap between `CommandSpec` (210 confirm-required) and `DASHBOARD_EXECUTION_CONFIRM_ACTIONS` (59) for planner-routed actions only. Re-derives rather than carrying a boolean across three hops, because a dropped field fails open on a T2; fails closed on an unknown action; detector traffic byte-identical (that is `e2e-bug.405`, a product decision). Checked and rejected the theory that the 151 are over-declared reads — 4 of 6 candidates were my own false positives. **Recovery delta unmeasured** (needs a 121-prompt replay), and note that 24 unblocked rejections are 24 commands that now *ask*, not 24 more completed. Unblocks `e2e-bug.399`. See AI-ROADMAP §129
- [~] `e2e-bug.399` **[Measured 2026-08-09 — twice, both negative]** — the planner is asked for entity ids, forbidden from guessing them, and never told a resolver exists (`resolver` appears **zero times** in the generated prompt); it correctly refuses. The deadlock is real. **Rendering the resolver is not the fix.** §120 measured it at 28.7% vs 30.6% and blamed `decidePlannerRoute`'s confirm gate; §129 removed that gate and §130 re-measured at **30.1%**, against baselines of **30.6%** and **32.4%**. The change is real — 13-14 of 121 prompts move verdict against a **3-prompt** baseline-vs-baseline noise floor — but it reshuffles failures rather than fixing them (5 prompts move `not_executable` -> `not_single_step`, the added rule making the model split work it then cannot route). **"Blocked on e2e-bug.404" is withdrawn.** Needs a different mechanism: resolution at execute time, not instruction at prompt time. **Now sized without traffic weighting:** `unresolved_notes` is 102 of 343 held-out spec examples (§141), ~30 points of planner reach. See AI-ROADMAP §130, §141
- [x] `e2e-bug.398` **[Fixed 2026-08-08]** — `PLAN_OUTPUT_CONTRACT` showed `"confidence": 0.0-1.0`, which is not valid JSON and not a value; the model emitted **exactly 0** for 14 of 18 sub-threshold steps and the 0.6 gate rejected them. Replaced with a concrete `0.9` plus one explanatory sentence: steps below the gate **18 → 8**, steps at zero **14 → 0**, and the survivors are genuine uncertainty. Third contract-literal bug in this string after `e2e-bug.388`. See AI-ROADMAP §99
- [x] `e2e-bug.397` **[Fixed 2026-08-08]** — **not a Phase 4 problem after all.** The planner shortlist rendered variables as bare names (`required: catalogDraft`), never showing the type that was in the spec all along, so the model answered an `object` with a sentence 18 times. Recursive type rendering took variable rejections **28 → 4**, and all 4 survivors are information genuinely absent from the prompt. End-to-end recovery moved only 25.5% → 27.8%, because §95 measured three overlapping causes and clearing one leaves the others. **§95's claim that Phase 4 gates Phase 8's mutating half is withdrawn** — it was a rendering bug. See AI-ROADMAP §98
- [~] `e2e-bug.396` **[Partial 2026-08-08]** — retrieval **55% → 63%** truth-in-shortlist across two passes: example-mining with a provenance filter (§96, +6) and a lexical identity bonus for the long tail (§97, +3, zero regressions). Weight 0.25 chosen over the higher-scoring 0.5 because 0.5 broke a prompt that previously worked and the module's stated invariant is that losing the right command is worse than keeping a wrong one. **Still open: 45 prompts.** Both levers are now spent — the remaining tail needs a different approach, not more tuning. See AI-ROADMAP §96, §97
- [x] `e2e-bug.403` **[Fixed 2026-08-08]** — `IntentCandidate.precedence` (default 0) is ranked above confidence, and only a planner route in a retired domain sets it. This is the missing half of §93's coupling: locking a domain against rescue without ranking the planner above the classifier hands those prompts to the classifier's *rejected* answer. `tour` **76% → 97%**, back to its §92 figure and again the one slice clearing 90%; every other domain unchanged to the trace. Deliberately **not** fixed by raising §99's confidence example — that wins the comparison instead of admitting it is invalid. See AI-ROADMAP §119
- [ ] `e2e-bug.406` **[Open]** — **§42's 90% retirement bar cannot be met by any Phase 8 slice, and that is a property of the corpus, not the planner.** 95% Wilson intervals on the per-domain table: `tour` 33/34 is **85-99%**, `push` 15/18 is 61-94% — no lower bound reaches 90%. Structurally, a 95% lower bound at 90% needs **n >= 35 traces with zero failures**; the largest slice has 34 traces and one failure, so it cannot clear the bar even scoring perfectly. The bar comes from §42, where it gates against the 8,509-case corpus and is a sharp line; applied to 6-34 rescue-dependent traces it is a precise instrument on data that cannot resolve it. More data is not obtainable — traffic stopped 2026-08-03 and the whole rescue-dependent population is 216 traces. Needs either an interval-based threshold or, better, a comparative criterion (planner recovery vs detector-deletion loss), which is what actually decided `tour`. Does not overturn §93. See AI-ROADMAP §128
- [ ] `e2e-bug.395` **[Open]** — **only 1 of 14 remaining Phase 8 slices is ready.** Measured across all 121 rescue-dependent prompts (216 traces): `tour` 97%, `push` 83%, everything else 0-18%, total **25.5%**. The dominant failure is **no route — 76 of 121**, not wrong answers (catalog: 20 of 22 prompts produce no routable plan). Per-slice tuning does not scale; §84-§88 spent four sections on `tour`'s retrieval alone. The remaining thirteen domains are blocked on one shared problem and need one global fix, not thirteen investigations. See AI-ROADMAP §94
- [ ] `e2e-bug.394` **[Open]** — Phase 8's exit says "zero `legacy_paraphrase` remaining", but the 15 tour/guide detectors **cannot be physically deleted**: 128 references across 31 files, and most are non-slice detectors using them as *negative guards* (`ai-confirm-my-booking-details`, `ai-list-my-upcoming-appointments`, `ai-explain-preparation-notes`, `ai-add-booking-to-calendar` and ~12 more). Removing the predicates removes the guards and lets those detectors compete for tour/guide prompts — a behaviour change §92 never measured. The guard call sites must migrate to a non-detector disambiguation before any slice can actually be deleted. §93 retired the slice *behaviourally* via `RESCUE_ACTION_LOCKED_DOMAINS` instead

- [x] `e2e-bug.389` **[Fixed 2026-08-08]** — `validatePlan` refused an entire plan whenever `plan.unresolved` was non-empty, with nothing recorded in `problems`, so a correctly routed read plus an unmappable modifier ("with pax and remaining capacity") became a reasonless rejection. - [x] `e2e-bug.364` **[Fixed 2026-08-08 — §81]** — `extractTimeSlotFromPrompt` returned 03:30 for "at 3:30 pm". **Three variants found**: (1) bare-24h branch ran before am/pm, its trailing `\b` satisfied by the space; (2) the clinic-lab copy only handled a meridiem when preceded by "at", so "3:30 pm" fell through; (3) the trailing `\b` could never match "p.m." — a boundary needs a word char and the pattern ends in a period — so dotted forms returned 06:45. All fixed, both copies, with regression tests.
- [x] `e2e-bug.363` **[Fixed 2026-08-08 — §81]** — `resolveTomorrowDateKey` computed `now + 24h` then `.toISOString()` (the UTC date), so every timezone was a day late in its local evening. Now delegates to §30`s `localCalendarDate` + `addCalendarDays` and **requires** a timeZone — a default is what let callers reintroduce it. Both duplicate copies fixed; the compound call site had `timeZone` in scope one line below.
- [~] `e2e-bug.362` **[Partially fixed 2026-08-08]** — the loose-matching half is fixed; the tie half is `e2e-bug.367`'s migration. Running the ticket's table first showed **two of its four rows do not reproduce** (exact and contains-tiers win before the ones it blamed). The real defect: `lower.includes(name)` matched letters inside words — employee `Al` resolved from **"is the salon open"** (s-al-on) and from "book Alice for a haircut". Fixed with Unicode word boundaries, and a test for Armenian names then exposed **the same defect in tier 4** (`Ան` from `Աննա`), which the ticket never named. Ties are untouched: they need `resolveEntity`'s ask-don't-pick, which changes the `T | undefined` contract at 27 call sites. See AI-ROADMAP §111
- [~] `e2e-bug.361` **[Triaged 2026-08-09]** — the customer surface carries 69% of traffic at 60.6% completion / 31.1% failure, and it is **not uniformly weak: ten commands carry 53.5% of all its failures**. `claim_referral_code` 96/96 failed, `reschedule_my_booking` 93.5%, `find_soonest_appointment` 87.1%, `confirm_my_booking_details` 76.1%, `compound_intent` 65.7%, `pay_online` 60.3% — against healthy neighbours on the same surface (`check_availability` 3.9%, `list_services` 12.3%). Bringing just those ten to 90% moves the surface **60.6% -> 83.9%**. `failure_reason` cannot help: its migration is dated 2026-08-15 and traffic stopped 2026-08-03 (same shape as the §124 `result_summary` near-miss). One of the ten is fixed (`e2e-bug.410`); nine remain. **Rates are upper bounds** — `e2e-bug.411` found clarifications recorded as failures and history cannot be recomputed. **Volume weighting is not demand** — `e2e-bug.416`. See AI-ROADMAP §133, §134, §139
- [ ] `e2e-bug.360` **[Open]** — **8 of 20** documented `CommandSpec.examples` never reach their own command's detector ("what packages do we sell", "stop offering hot stone massage", "Sarah paid cash for today's massage"). Meanwhile **0 of 116** surface-form variants (casing/whitespace/punctuation/politeness) break. The detector layer is brittle to *phrasing*, not to *surface form* — so input normalisation will not help, and these are Phase 8 planner-handover acceptance cases — [`TODO.md` e2e-bug.360](./TODO.md#e2e-bug360--8-of-20-documented-commandspec-examples-never-reach-their-own-commands-detector--open)
- [x] `e2e-bug.400` **[Fixed 2026-08-08]** — it was not two gates duplicating work, it was one suite existing only to fail. `ai-command-eval.spec.ts` asserted `failed === 0` over a corpus with **404 known failures** (`e2e-bug.358` fixed the *gate* to ratchet that debt; this assertion was never updated), so it had been permanently red while re-running the whole 8,464-case corpus in its own process — **355s, 11.5% of the AI sweep**. Deleted: suite **355s → 15s**, one fewer permanently-red suite. The accuracy gate asserts strictly more over the same cases. See AI-ROADMAP §102
- [ ] `e2e-bug.359` **[Open]** — `modules/ai` has 1,268 spec files and a full sweep does not finish in a working session (9 saturated jest workers). This is why Phase 2's "red means red" has never been done: observing the failure set costs tens of minutes, so nobody iterates, nobody notices new failures, and proving a change safe needs ~2× that via stash-and-diff. Fix the runtime first (ts-jest transform, sharding, heaviest-suite audit) — [`TODO.md` e2e-bug.359](./TODO.md#e2e-bug359--the-ai-test-sweep-takes-tens-of-minutes-which-is-why-red-means-red-has-never-been-fixed--open)
- [~] `e2e-bug.358` **[Partially fixed 2026-08-10]** — 24 intents lost 49 previously-passing cases while the accuracy gate was permanently red. **`lookup_customer` fixed: 0/6 → 6/6**, two independent faults. (1) The rescue branch that sets `bookingContext`/`date` sat *after* `tryRescueDashboardCustomerRead`, whose `isLookupCustomerPrompt` arm returns only `customerName` — so it was unreachable for the prompts it was written for; four cases had the right action and failed only on params. Extracted as `lookupCustomerWithBookingContext` and shared by both sites. (2) `rescueExplainProviderAvailabilityIntent` claimed "summarize customer X who has a booking with Y at Z". **Three attempts, each scored against the 8,464-case corpus:** reordering the chain got 6/6 but cost `admin_delete_customer_data` and `explain_any_provider_option` a case each; narrowing it got 4/6 and still cost one; guarding inside the util itself got **6/6 with no collateral** and covers all five call sites. Corpus **95.23% → 95.3%**, no regressed intents; manifest **457 → 445**. Phase 8 freeze 545 → 544 — a matcher became a guard (`routing_shape`), recorded. Then `e2e-bug.424` found the far larger cluster: nine intents at 0% across 241 cases, one cause. **Remaining: 147 deterministic failures.** See AI-ROADMAP §148
- [x] `e2e-bug.357` **[Fixed 2026-08-07]** — ~~AI requests carry no conversation id~~ The premise was wrong: `AiCommandDto.history` is replayed *in full* on every request, so the first user message is stable and a key is derivable server-side. §50 added `deriveConversationIdentity`; §51 added `ai_command_trace.session_id` + `session_turn`, migrated and indexed, populated through `buildGatewayCommandTraceInput`. No client change was needed. Anonymous visitors keep a null id by design, since hashing business + text alone would merge two strangers — [`TODO.md` e2e-bug.357](./TODO.md#e2e-bug357--ai-requests-carry-no-conversation-id-so-follow-ups-cannot-be-resolved-server-side--open)
- [x] `e2e-bug.355` **[Fixed 2026-08-08]** — the dead flat permission model is deleted: `COMMAND_REGISTRY[].tiers`, `isIntentAllowedForTier`, `AiCommandRegistryService.allowedForTier` and `AiGatewayService.assertIntentAllowed` formed a closed loop with **no production callers**, verified before removal. Its 425-of-3,492 disagreements are now unrepresentable rather than merely documented, and the conformance test that pinned them is gone with it. §104's red-means-red gate caught the one reader I missed (`ai-product-guide.integration.spec.ts`) in a suite the fast chain does not run — one change after the gate was built. See AI-ROADMAP §105
- [x] `e2e-bug.356` **[Fixed 2026-08-08]** — the dashboard and provider gates now read `CommandSpec.tiers`, which fails closed, and fall back to the deny-lists only for actions no spec covers (pipeline pseudo-actions). Measured first: **no spec = 0** across every registry command on both surfaces, so this was a switch, not a migration. One behavioural delta, a tightening — `dashboard/client` went from **265 commands allowed to 0**, removing the reliance on `AiGatewayService`'s blanket refusal that was load-bearing rather than redundant. A command added tomorrow with no tier entry is now denied. See AI-ROADMAP §100

## Full index (all 253)

> **Two sessions have been appending to this file concurrently (2026-08-18/19), and ticket ids
> collided three times** — `476`, `477`, then `482`, each taken by both sessions within minutes
> because both computed `max + 1` against a copy of the file the other was already extending.
> Resolved by moving the later-written duplicates (now `483`, `484`); `259` and `434` were already
> duplicated before either session started. **If you are allocating a new id, re-read the file
> immediately before writing and take `max + 1` then** — a number chosen at the start of a slice is
> stale by the time the row is appended. The same collision hit `§` section numbers in `TODO.md`,
> where §197–§209 are used twice; those are not resolved, because renumbering either series would
> break the other's code comments.
