| `e2e-bug.497` | **Fixed 2026-08-25** | yes | **Every scheduled job now runs once per cluster instead of once per instance.** The count in the original filing was stale: **11 `@Cron` methods across 9 files**, with *zero* locking of any kind. Several send things people can see — appointment reminders, marketing re-engagement, rebooking nudges, end-of-day push, HITL escalations — so a second instance means a second send. Added `SchedulerLockService.runExclusively(jobKey, work)` using **Postgres advisory locks**, chosen over a locks table because they need no schema, no migration and no cleanup: the lock dies with the session that took it, including when the instance is killed mid-job, whereas a table lock has to model stale-lock expiry and inter-instance clock skew to get the same property — and getting that subtly wrong reintroduces the duplicate. Taken on a **dedicated `QueryRunner`**, because advisory locks are session-scoped while TypeORM pools connections, so `dataSource.query()` could put lock and unlock on different sessions. `pg_try_advisory_lock` (non-blocking) so a losing instance skips rather than queues — the next tick is already scheduled. **Fails closed**: if exclusivity cannot be established the job is skipped and logged at error, because running anyway is the duplicate this prevents, and nothing real is lost (the lock is a trivial round-trip that only fails when Postgres is down, which every one of these jobs needs anyway). The key is hashed **in TypeScript, not via `hashtext()`** — that Postgres function is an undocumented internal, and with fail-closed semantics its absence would silently stop every scheduled job rather than duplicate one. Wiring shape keeps `@Cron` on a thin new `*Scheduled` wrapper and leaves each work method's name and body untouched, so all 5 existing scheduler specs pass unchanged. **Guarded**: a structural test asserts every `@Cron` body delegates through the lock and that no two jobs share a key (negative-controlled — adding an unlocked job fails it by name). 15 new tests; gate exact-match at 44 suites / 84 tests |
| `e2e-bug.435` | **Open — hardening, no measured instance** | no | wrong-surface rescue shadows the correct one and yields `none`; ~163 rescues share the shape. **Measured 2026-08-21 and re-scoped from severe to hardening.** Across the **8,464-case deterministic corpus the failure mode does not occur**: the single remaining failure is `e2e137-professional-profile` (e2e-bug.456, a product decision), not a surface shadow. The hazard is real — each phase is first-match-wins and `acceptRescueForSurface` discards afterwards — but a ~163-site refactor on zero evidenced instances is not proportionate, and this session has repeatedly found such premises overstated. **What was done instead:** pinned the protection that already exists. A wrong-surface *classified* hit falls through to the unknown phase (e2e-bug.77), and that lived in a bare `if` with no test, so it would have regressed silently; `ai-e2e435-rescue-chain-fallthrough.spec.ts` now covers it, plus the deliberate no-fallthrough case and the residual hazard (both candidates illegal ⇒ nothing) pinned as current-not-desired. **Before any refactor, get an instance** — either a corpus case or a real trace — because the fix's shape depends on whether the shadowing happens across phases (cheap) or within one (expensive). **Instances found 2026-08-21, and they were in the known-failures manifest, not the corpus.** The zero-instance measurement above was taken against the 8,464-case corpus; the manifest is where they live. `ai-e2e248-reschedule-nearest.util` (7 tests) and `ai-e2e267-reschedule-semicolon.util` (5) are the same case twice: prompts like *"**Reschedule** Anna appointment to the soonest free slot tomorrow"* and *"Move Gevorg's appointment on June 1 to June 2 nearest free time"*, entering with `fromAction: 'create_booking'` and expected to be corrected to `reschedule_booking`. **The detector is innocent** — probed directly, `isRescheduleExistingAppointmentPrompt` is `true` and `isFindSoonestAppointmentPrompt` is `false` for all three prompts — yet `rescue()` returns `action: 'create_booking'`, i.e. a rescue fires and yields the wrong action while the correct one sits behind it. That is precisely the shadowing this ticket describes, with **12 reproducible instances**. They also mean these 12 are **not** the product decision e2e-bug.499 classified them as: a prompt containing the literal word "Reschedule" routing to `create_booking` is not a defensible product choice. **FIXED 2026-08-23 — and it needed no refactor.** Reproducing the rescue calls named the thief outright: `rescueReason: 'create_booking_not_employee'`. `tryRescueCreateBookingNotEmployee` trips on these prompts because they name an employee *and* ask for a slot, so it fired first and shadowed the `reschedule_pattern` rescue that handles the same prompt correctly further down the chain — in production, leaving the existing appointment alone and creating a second one. **6 of the 8 scenarios already resolved correctly**, so the fix is a guard on the thief (`if (isRescheduleExistingAppointmentPrompt(prompt)) return null;`), not a change to the reschedule path, whose detector was innocent throughout. 13 manifest entries cleared (7 + 5 + one in `ai-intent-rescue.service.spec.ts` literally named *"rescues move appointment as reschedule before create_booking"*). Corpus unchanged at 99.99% with `create_booking` 7/7 and `book_nearest_slot` 6/6 intact; 27 gates green. **The general shadowing hazard remains open** — this was one instance, fixed at its site; the chain is still first-match-wins |
| `e2e-bug.436` | **Closed 2026-08-21 — fixed by the registry generation** | yes | rescue routes a dashboard owner to customer-surface commands the spec forbids; `my_gift_cards` **executed** there. **Verified closed 2026-08-21, and the cause is worth recording because nobody fixed it deliberately.** `acceptRescueForSurface` always called `isIntentAllowedOnSurface` — but that reads `COMMAND_REGISTRY`, which was hand-maintained and disagreed with the specs, listing these as dashboard-legal. **§161–§164 made the registry generate *from* `CommandSpec`**, so the two can no longer disagree. Probed directly: `my_gift_cards`, `my_subscriptions` and `loyalty_points_balance` all report `dashboard=false, customer=true`, and the gate now returns `null` for each on dashboard. Pinned by `ai-e2e436-rescue-surface-gate.spec.ts` (8 tests), because a fix that emerged from two unrelated pieces of work is exactly the kind that regresses silently. **One hole deliberately left and pinned as current behaviour:** `acceptRescueForSurface` opens with `if (!surface) return result`, so an **absent** surface accepts everything — the same fail-open default that made e2e-bug.444 look like a live steal. The fix above therefore depends on every caller passing its surface; the production path does |
| `e2e-bug.437` | **Closed 2026-08-21 — already fixed, now pinned** | no | `summarizeExecutionModes`'s `byReason` record omits `execution_unproven`, so that bucket increments from `undefined` and the summary reports `NaN` | **Verified 2026-08-21: the key is present and no bucket reports NaN.** `byReason` initialises all five `ProposeOnlyReason` variants including `execution_unproven`; compared the union against the literal programmatically — **no missing keys**. **Pinned with a runtime exhaustiveness test rather than left to the compiler**, because the failure mode is silent arithmetic: `byReason[reason] += 1` on an absent key increments `undefined`, and `undefined + 1` is `NaN`, which TypeScript will not catch once any `as` is in the path — and this function is already called with `as any` in its own spec. Two tests: every counter is a number and never NaN, and a reason that actually fires increments to a real total |

| `e2e-bug.439` | **Fixed 2026-08-21** | no | `explain_tour_services`' completion validator is `isExplainTourServicesPrompt(p) ? [] : []` — both branches empty, so the predicate is evaluated and discarded | **Fixed:** the branch is now `explain_tour_services: () => []` with a comment saying why. The old form evaluated `isExplainTourServicesPrompt` and discarded the answer, so it read as an unfinished validator rather than a deliberate "this read has no required fields". **Kept as an explicit entry rather than deleted** — `validateCommand` does `rule ? rule(cmd) : []`, so an absent entry behaves identically, but the entry records that the question was asked and answered and gives a future required field somewhere to go. The now-unused import was dropped; the predicate itself is still used by `ai-checkout-recommendations.util.ts` and `ai-explain-preparation-notes.util.ts`, so nothing was orphaned — detector inventory unchanged at 772. 13 suites green (249 tests); 27 gates green; corpus 99.99% |
| `e2e-bug.440` | **Fixed 2026-08-21 — premise corrected** | no | a spec's `handler` records where the switch case lives, not where params are read — **5 of 16** commands across two C2 slices point at the wrong service (`no_show_recovery`, `mark_no_shows`, `fill_slot_from_waitlist`, `apply_schedule`, `configure_ai_autopilot`) | **`handler` is not documentation — it is a derivation key, and that is why the obvious fix does not work.** `deriveApiModule` and `deriveCompoundStep` both index `HANDLER_*` maps by it, and an unmapped value makes the generator **throw** — proved by changing one spec and watching it fail with *"Cannot derive registry entry for mark_no_shows: unknown handler AiBookingCoreService"*. So relabelling the five required extending both maps first. **The extension is inert by construction:** `AiBookingCoreService` and `AiMetaOpsService` were added with the *same* values their sibling handlers already carry (`'ai-command'` / `true`), so every derived registry entry is byte-identical and only the label changed. Corrected: `no_show_recovery` (`AiOperationsService` → `AiBookingCoreService` — the operations copy takes already-resolved `bookingIds`, so params are read upstream), `mark_no_shows` and `fill_slot_from_waitlist` (→ `AiBookingCoreService`), `apply_schedule` (→ `AiScheduleHandlersService`), `configure_ai_autopilot` (→ `AiMetaOpsService`). 27 gates green; corpus unchanged at 99.99%. The one failing registry-integration assertion (`public-booking` vs `provider-mobile`) is **pre-existing and in the manifest** — confirmed by reverting and re-running |

| `e2e-bug.442` | **Fixed 2026-08-21** | yes | `configure_openai_integration` **and** `configure_whatsapp_integration` return raw credentials to the client — both put `patch: parsed` in details (`apiKey` / `accessToken`), `patch` is not in `INTERNAL_COMMAND_DETAIL_KEYS`, and neither key is `_`-prefixed, so `sanitizeCommandDetailsForClient` passes them through verbatim | **Fixed by stripping `patch` as a category, not by redacting the two field names.** `'patch'` is now in `INTERNAL_COMMAND_DETAIL_KEYS`, so `sanitizeCommandDetailsForClient` removes it from every command's client-facing details. Verified the credential really was in there: `parsed` is typed `Pick<UpdateOpenAiIntegrationDto, 'usePlatformDefault' | 'apiKey'>` and the spec's own rules describe `apiKey` as "plaintext sk-… — encrypted server-side", i.e. still plaintext at this point. **Category over blacklist on purpose:** redacting `apiKey`/`accessToken` by name would leave the next credential-bearing patch to leak, the same blacklist-chases-an-open-set failure as e2e-bug.446. Safe to remove wholesale — `patch` is an echo of what the caller just sent, nothing in `backend/src` or `frontend/src` reads `details.patch`, and the user-facing result is `settings`, which is untouched (pinned by a test). 4 tests, negative-controlled (3 of 4 fail with the leak restored); 8 related suites green (112 tests); 27 gates green; corpus unchanged at 99.99% |
| `e2e-bug.443` | **Fixed 2026-08-21** | yes | clinical patient lookup scans an arbitrary, unordered `take: 200` slice of customers and ignores `isActive` — past 200 customers a real patient silently fails to resolve, and a deactivated one can match. **Fixed:** `resolvePatientClinicalCustomer` (`ai-patient-clinical-mutations.util.ts`) now filters by name **in SQL** — `{ businessId, isActive: true, name: ILike('%…%') }` with `order: { name, id }` — so the cap bounds *matching* rows instead of arbitrary ones, deactivated customers are excluded by the database, and repeated calls agree. All three faults were in the query; the in-memory exact-beats-substring tie-break below it was always fine and is unchanged. LIKE metacharacters in the name are escaped, so a customer called `50%_off` matches literally rather than as a wildcard. 4 tests asserting the query itself, negative-controlled (4 of 23 fail when reverted). **Not addressed, and worth its own decision:** when several active patients match by substring, this still takes the first in a now-stable order rather than refusing the tie — on a path that writes to a patient chart, that is the D5 question on the highest-stakes surface |
| `e2e-bug.444` | **Fixed 2026-08-21 — premise corrected** | yes | "Show my profile" rescues to `show_provider_profile` instead of the customer's own `my_profile` — a customer asking for their own profile is routed to a provider-directory read; `ai-customer-crm.integration.spec.ts` has been red on this independently of C2 | **The steal is not reachable in production, and the ticket's framing was wrong.** The guard already exists — `blockProviderNests = surface === 'customer' || 'public'` (e2e-bug.77), whose comment names `show_provider_profile` explicitly — and the production caller, `command-understanding-pipeline.service.ts:669`, **always passes `surface: input.surface`**. So a real customer prompt was never routed to the provider-directory read. **What was actually broken was the spec:** `ai-customer-crm.integration.spec.ts` called `rescue.rescue({prompt, action, params})` with **no surface**, and an absent surface is the *permissive* value, so the provider nests stayed eligible. Fixed by having the spec declare `surface: 'customer'`, which is what it was always testing. **Isolated, not assumed**: with the spec change alone the assertion passes, proving the service was never at fault. **Also forwarded `surface` through the `action === 'unknown'` re-entry** in `disambiguateMisclassified`, which dropped it — but that branch is **unreachable** (it would recurse into `rescue()` forever; probed with an unclaimed prompt, returns `null` without overflowing), so it is defensive only and is commented as such rather than sold as a fix. **Revealed a second, previously-masked failure in the same test** — filed as e2e-bug.493 |
| `e2e-bug.445` | **Fixed 2026-08-19 (§221) — the branch was cosmetic; the missing coverage was the defect** | no | `command-completion.pipeline.service.ts:224` — `service: services.length === 1 ? services[0] : services[0]`, both branches identical, so the guard is dead; but `services.length > 1` means *several services were named*, not an ambiguous match, so the obvious "fix" would break multi-service booking. **Confirmed §221, and the reasoning in this ticket was right where the tests were not.** Applying the obvious fix (`length === 1 ? services[0] : undefined`, matching the `employee` line above) broke **nothing**: 40 suites / 537 tests around the pipeline all passed, and the 8,464-case corpus was unmoved at 99.99% / +0%. That is a coverage gap, not a safety proof — `enrichedParams.serviceId` is set **only** when exactly one service resolves (`command-completion.pipeline.service.ts`, `if (services.length === 1)`), so with the singular withheld the completeness check `entities.service || enrichedParams.serviceId` has nothing left to be true and the validator asks for a service the user already named several of. The step intent was added to a new `CATALOG_COMPOUND_EXTRA_STEP_INTENTS`, deliberately **not** to `CATALOG_INTENTS`, because that list *is* `DASHBOARD_CATALOG_DISPATCH_INTENTS` and widening it would route the action to the catalog service, which does not handle it. Russian masculine: `RUSSIAN_CASE_SUFFIX` (e2e-bug.481) — `ом|ем|а|у|е`, ending must end the word, gated on Cyrillic plus a 3-character minimum stem. Russian feminine: **out of scope by construction, not by omission** — «Мария»→«Марии» is a stem change, so the name is not a prefix of the inflected form and no suffix rule can reach it; pinned as an explicit known-miss test rather than left implicit. The tension this entry described with e2e-bug.446 — fixing one breaking the other — is resolved: both are closed together |

| `e2e-bug.449` | **Closed 2026-08-25** | no | **The gates the roadmap treats as blocking now run in CI.** The enforcement half was wired earlier (the `ai-gates` job runs `test:ai-roadmap-gates` and `test:ai-accuracy`; `ai-known-failures` runs the sweep across 4 shards with a merge job doing the single exact comparison). The half that remained was the flip side of the same problem: **`backend-tests` ran `npm test`, which cannot pass while the manifest is non-empty**, so it was red on every push — a required check that is red by construction gates nothing and trains everyone to read a red X as normal, which is precisely when a real regression slips by. **Retired** (owner's decision; the roadmap had deliberately written this up rather than made it, since it changes how everyone's PRs are checked). Redundancy verified rather than assumed: `npm test` is bare `jest`, the sweep runs the same binary with `--testPathPatterns=src/` against `rootDir: src` — so both select every backend spec — with the same default parallelism and the same ts-jest transforms, and production typechecking is `npm run typecheck` in `ai-gates`, stricter than anything `npm test` did. The only difference was the verdict: *"84 tests are red"* every run, versus *"the red set changed"*. **Owner follow-up:** remove "Backend tests" from branch protection's required checks — GitHub blocks merges waiting on a required check that no longer reports |
  **Measured 2026-08-17 (§191), and it is worse than "the gate never runs": CI's backend job fails on every push.** `npm test` is plain `jest` with no ignore patterns, so it runs the whole backend — including the 203 failures the `test:ai-known-failures` manifest exists to tolerate. Run exactly as `ci.yml` does (`CI=true`, `NODE_OPTIONS=--max-old-space-size=4096`): **94 failed suites, 285 failed tests, 1,923 suites, 870 s.** There is no `continue-on-error` on the step. So the backend job has been red continuously, which also explains how 89 ungated non-AI failures (`e2e-bug.471`) and a broken PHI read path (`e2e-bug.472`) went unnoticed — a permanently red check reports nothing.
  **Steps 1 and 2 are done (§191 added the `ai-gates` job; §205 widened the manifest to the whole backend, so that job now gates all 1,928 suites). Step 3 remains: `backend-tests` still runs plain `jest` and still cannot pass — point it at the gate or retire it, which is a decision about everyone's PR checks.** **The fix is therefore not "add the gates to CI".** `npm test` cannot pass today, so adding `test:ai-roadmap-gates`/`test:ai-known-failures` beside it changes nothing until the 285 are triaged. The sequencing that actually works: (1) make CI run the gates that *are* green today — `test:ai-roadmap-gates`, `test:ai-known-failures`, `test:ai-accuracy` — as their own job, so something real is enforced immediately; (2) extend the known-failures manifest to the whole backend (`e2e-bug.471`) so `npm test` becomes gateable; (3) only then make the `npm test` step meaningful. Step 1 is additive and cannot break anything that is not already broken. Blocks the sharding work in F1, which needs a CI job to shard. **Related CI gap found in the same pass:** `cache: npm` on all four `ci.yml` jobs is `setup-node`'s *dependency* cache; nothing persists jest's transform cache and no `cacheDirectory` is set, so every CI run pays a measured **56 s** cold-cache penalty — and every future shard would pay it in full, capping an 8-way shard at ~3.2× |

Found while fixing the D1 `explain_guest_checkout_fields` steal; **pre-existing and unaffected by that change** — the `\bbook\b` gate blocks it either way. Pinned as current behaviour in `ai-rebook-last-appointment.util.spec.ts`. Low severity: `rebook_last_appointment` scores 24/24 on the corpus, so canonical phrasings are fine, and this is a natural-phrasing gap rather than a broken path. Fix is likely one alternative in `EXPLICIT_REBOOK_CUE` for `same\s+\w+\s+as\s+last`, but it needs corpus scoring — widening a rebook cue is exactly how the guest-checkout steal happened | **Fixed:** `EXPLICIT_REBOOK_CUE` now allows one bounded noun between `same` and `as last` (`same\s+(?!<contact field>)\w{2,20}\s+as\s+last`). **The exclusion is the point** — without it, "use the same email as last time" would become an explicit rebook cue, which is e2e-bug.362's guest-checkout steal reintroduced through the very cue that was widened; the carve-out reuses the same contact-field list as `hasPriorVisitCue`. | `e2e-bug.466` | **Fixed 2026-08-19 (§218)** | no | **A marketing date range built from the `dateRange` enum includes the last day; the same range built from the prompt excludes it.** In `ai-marketing-growth.logic.ts` the enum branches closed on `23:59:59.999` of the end day while the prompt branch closed at that day's midnight, so `Between(from, to)` silently drops the final day's rows — "this month" typed as free text under-reports registrations by one day versus the identical request arriving as `dateRange: 'this_month'`. Both branches now share the canonical resolver (§186) but the two closing conventions were **deliberately preserved**, because changing what a report counts is a behaviour change that does not belong in a dedup slice. Decide which is correct — almost certainly end-of-day for both — then collapse them. Note the same file's `from` is anchored at `T00:00:00.000Z`, so a fully correct fix converts local day boundaries to instants using `_timeZone` rather than pinning to UTC, matching what §186 fixed for the range itself. **Fixed §218, both halves.** The closing convention is unified on end-of-day, so `dateRange: 'this_month'` and "this month" typed as prose now return byte-identical windows — pinned by a test that asserts the two against each other rather than against a literal, because the failure mode was disagreement, not a wrong constant. The bounds are also converted with `getUtcBoundsForDateKey(dayKey, timeZone)` instead of being pinned to `T00:00:00.000Z`: `resolveDateRange` had already computed the day keys in `_timeZone`, so re-anchoring them in UTC slid the whole window by the offset — for Asia/Yerevan (UTC+4) the range ran 2026-08-01T00:00Z…2026-08-31T00:00Z when it should have run 2026-07-31T20:00Z…2026-08-31T19:59:59.999Z. **Found while fixing it:** the shared `getUtcBoundsForDateKey` closed each day on `23:59:59.000`, so all **twelve** of its callers — every one of which uses it as "the whole of day X" against an inclusive `Between` — silently dropped anything in the final second of the day. Widened to `.999`; that direction can only include rows that were always meant to be in range, and the three suites among those callers that fail (`e2e318`, two `public-booking-tour`) fail identically with and without the change, so they are `e2e-bug.471`'s pre-existing set and not fallout |
| `e2e-bug.467` | **Open — the live half is fixed (§217); the shape decision is measured as inert** | no | **`params.dateRange` carries two incompatible shapes under one name.** The util parsers (`ai-marketing-growth.util.ts:611`, `ai-retail-finance.util.ts:338/952`, `ai-integrations.util.ts:546`) set it to a **string enum** (`'this_month'`, `'this_week'`, and `'this_quarter'` in `ai-retail-finance-dashboard-classifier.fixtures.ts`), while `operational-plan-builder.service.ts:988/998` populates it from `meta.dateRange`, typed `{ start: string; end: string }` — an **object**. Any consumer testing `params.dateRange === 'this_month'` is dead code for the object producer and vice versa; `dateRange` is not a declared variable in any `CommandSpec`, so nothing reconciles them. Found in §186 while migrating the two local `resolveDateRange` copies. `'this_quarter'` is additionally produced but handled by neither the old marketing branches nor the canonical resolver, so it currently falls through to prompt parsing. Decide on one shape, declare it, and let the resolver own the conversion. **Measured 2026-08-19 (§217), and the premise needs correcting: the two shapes never meet.** They are separate namespaces that share a key name. The **object** shape runs plan/workflow only — `operational-plan-builder.service.ts:988/998` writes it, `buildNoShowRecoveryPlanSteps` declares it explicitly as `dateRange?: { start: string; end: string }`, and `resolveDirectScheduleDateKeys` consumes it behind a `range?.start && range?.end` guard. The **string** shape runs prompt-parsed reads only: 8 writers, and exactly one reader, `resolveMarketingDateRange` via `DATE_RANGE_PHRASES`. No command reaches both, and each consumer degrades safely on the other's value (a string has no `.start`; an object indexes the phrase map as `[object Object]`). So the ticket's 'dead code for the other producer' is true and **inert** — a latent trap, not a live defect, and the shape decision can wait. It is also already declared where it is read: `marketing.summarize_new_registrations` declares `dateRange` as a string. **What was live, and is fixed in §217:** the enum emitted values nothing could resolve. `ai-retail-finance.util.ts` emits `'this_quarter'` and `'this_year'`; `DATE_RANGE_PHRASES` had only `this_month`/`this_week`, and the canonical `resolveDateRange` had **no quarter branch at all** — measured, `'this quarter'`, `'last quarter'` and `'this year'` all returned `null`, so "summarize P&L this quarter" silently used the caller's default window instead of the quarter asked for. Added quarter/year branches (computed from the month; dayjs' `quarterOfYear` plugin is not loaded in that file and loading one for three lines of arithmetic is the larger change) plus the two missing phrase keys. `'first_week'` from `ai-provider-onboarding-compound.util.ts` is still unreachable and deliberately left: 'first week' of what is an onboarding question, not a calendar one |
| `e2e-bug.468` | **Fixed 2026-08-17 (§187)** | yes | **"Reschedule my appointment to 6:45 p.m." moved the booking to 06:45 — twelve hours early, silently.** e2e-bug.364's third and fourth homes. `extractRescheduleTimeSlotFromPrompt`, `extractRescheduleTargetTime` (two branches) and `extractTimeSlotFromPrompt` each carried their own hand-written meridiem alternation, and every one of them was wrong in at least one form: three used `(am\|pm\|a\.m\.\|p\.m\.)\b`, and **a word boundary can never follow the period in `p.m.`**, so the whole meridiem branch was dead and the prompt fell through to a 24-hour reading. `extractTimeSlotFromPrompt` had been fixed for `p.m.` (§184 relied on it as the canonical) but still failed `p.m` without the trailing dot, because its alternation spelled the dots out instead of making them optional. **Why four copies shipped broken and no test caught it:** the existing AM/PM suite only ever used `AM`, `pm` and `3pm` — the three forms that always worked — and `a.m.` cannot regress, since its 12-hour and 24-hour readings coincide before noon. Only the p.m. forms could reveal it, and none were tested. **Fixed** by giving the pattern one home, `MERIDIEM_GROUP_SOURCE` in `ai-datetime-resolution.util.ts` (dots individually optional, `(?!\w)` never `\b`), spliced into all four sites and used by `resolveTimeOfDay` itself. 28 regression cases pin every dotted form across all four entry points, a.m. included, specifically so the coincidence cannot hide the next regression. See AI-ROADMAP §187 |
| `e2e-bug.469` | **Fixed 2026-08-18 (§211)** | no | **`extractTimeSlotFromPrompt` returns times that are not times.** Measured while fixing e2e-bug.468: `"at 24:00"` → `24:00`, `"at 99:99"` → `99:99`, `"at 003:30 pm"` → `42:00`. `normalizeTime24` pads and formats but never range-checks, so out-of-range input propagates as a well-formed-looking string into whatever consumes a time slot. `resolveTimeOfDay` rejects all three (`not_found`). Not fixed in §187 because that slice deliberately preserved the extractor's "always answer, never ask" contract and returning null here is a behaviour change for callers that currently receive a string. **That last sentence was wrong on both counts, and §211 fixed it elsewhere.** `normalizeTime24` **does** range-check (`hour >= 0 && hour <= 23 && minute >= 0 && minute <= 59`); when the check fails, its documented fallback is `return trimmed` — the input unchanged. Defensible for a *formatter*, wrong for an *extractor*. And **`42:00` did not come from it at all**: `parseAmPmClockTime` applies `h + 12` to whatever the regex captured, and `"at 003:30 pm"` reached it as hour **30**. So the value I cited as evidence was produced by a different function. **Fixed where the problem lives, leaving all 109 `normalizeTime24` callers untouched:** (1) `parseAmPmClockTime` now returns `string | null` and rejects hours outside 0-12 / minutes outside 0-59 — a 12-hour clock hour cannot be 30; (2) its four call sites (all one file, all inside `string | null` functions) capture and **fall through** instead of returning; (3) the two bare-24h branches validate the result via `asValidTime`. **The fall-through fixed something I had not noticed:** `"at 14:00 pm"` was `26:00` and now reads **14:00** — a meridiem on an already-24h time is noise, and the 24-hour branch reads it correctly once the am/pm branch stops answering unconditionally. Corpus **8463/8464, +0%**; 8 cases pinned including the valid forms that must not change. **Follow-on §219 — the shared util was still behind its own caller.** The extractor fix above is complete, but `normalizeTime24` in `common/utils/time-format.util.ts` had not learned the same rule, and its 12-hour branch had no upper bound: `'13:30 PM'` came back as **`'25:30'`**. That is worse than this ticket's original complaint, which was that the util *echoes* malformed input — echoing is deliberate and load-bearing (≈50 callers, an 'always return a string' contract, so `'24:00'` passes through for validators to refuse rather than being 'repaired' to `'00:00'` and silently booking midnight). Fabricating a plausible-looking `'25:30'` is the opposite failure and has no such defence. Guarded with `hour <= 12` and the meridiem stripped so the 24-hour branch reads the clock part, matching `extractTimeSlotFromPrompt('at 14:00 pm')` → `'14:00'`. Nine caller suites report an identical 7 failed / 12 tests with and without the guard. `time-format.util.ts` had **no spec file at all**, which is how this survived every one of those callers; added one, 13 tests |

But 17 of the 33 `update_employee` cases name people who are not on it, so the corpus asserts "this is an employee command" about names the roster says are strangers. Today nothing reads the roster during rescue, so the mismatch is invisible; the moment anything does, those 17 cases fail for a reason that is an artefact of the fixture rather than a defect in the code. Measured in §188 while attempting `e2e-bug.460`. **Enlarged by §190:** the fixture is now the smaller half. `input.employees` is not a roster on every surface — the **provider** app passes a single-element array built from the signed-in provider (`provider-ai-command.service.ts:747`) and the **customer** surface passes none at all, while dashboard and public pass the full active list. So any rule reading `input.employees` must declare which surfaces it is valid on; on provider, a "not a known employee" test would treat every colleague as a stranger. A per-case eval roster fixes the measurability problem but not this one.
  **§210 added the mechanism**: `AiCommandEvalCase.employees?` with the runner resolving `evalCase.employees ?? SAMPLE_EMPLOYEES` at **both** places a roster is supplied — `routeDeterministic` and `rescue`. Corpus **8463/8464, +0%**: defaults preserve behaviour exactly. **Stated plainly, it is inert today.** I tried and failed to construct a case whose outcome changes with the declared roster: `book me with Sofia tomorrow`, `move Sofia's 3pm to 4pm`, a two-name availability compound — all identical with and without a roster naming those people. **That inertness is the finding, not a defect in the change**: no rescue rule consults `employees` to decide anything, which is precisely why §188's roster rule had to be written from scratch and why it could not be scored. The remaining work is data, not plumbing — give the 17 `update_employee` cases in `ai-staff-operations.fixtures.ts` rosters that contain the people their prompts name, at the point a rule exists to read them. Doing it now would add fixture data no code reads. **Fix is a per-case roster** — let a case declare the employees it presupposes, defaulting to `SAMPLE_EMPLOYEES` — which is a prerequisite for `e2e-bug.460` and for any future rule that resolves a name against real data instead of guessing from wording |

  **Ungated total: 89 → 9 failures, 21 → 5 suites** (and the suite total now includes 106 tests that could not load before `e2e-bug.477`) (§193 UUID fixtures, §194 repo defaults, §196 payment repos). **78 of the 89 were stale test fixtures; no production code has been changed.** **All 9 remaining are genuine** — the mechanical class is exhausted: seeded-slot assertions (tour ×3), two `public-booking` count assertions (`e2e273`, `e2e318`), `e2e-bug.475`'s prepayment question (×3). `clinic-task-auto` closed as `e2e-bug.479`; `e2e-bug.478` fixed in §204. **78 of the 89 were stale fixtures and none needed a production change.** A **UUID guard** silently rerouting tests past the behaviour they assert has now been found on three separate surfaces (`isStaffMemberUserId` §192, `assertUuid` §193, `isUuid` §197) — the failure names the guard, or nothing, never the behaviour. Remaining: the `manager.transaction` group (needs per-harness wiring to seeded data), two one-off stale service mocks, and the genuine assertions — `e2e-bug.475`, `clinic-task-auto.util` (bound to real time), and two `public-booking` count assertions. The remaining bulk is one mechanical class (stale mocks) and a handful of real questions. **Nothing here should be written into a manifest as "known" until it has been read** — two of the first three suites examined were fixtures, not defects |
| `e2e-bug.472` | **Closed 2026-08-17 (§192) — my diagnosis was wrong; it was a stale test, not a PHI defect** | no | **Booking PHI is returned as raw ciphertext instead of being decrypted or masked.** 4 failures in `booking-phi-compliance.integration.spec.ts` and `booking-phi-minimum-access.integration.spec.ts`, both inside `e2e-bug.471`'s ungated set. One root cause, visible in both directions: staff *assigned* to the booking get `"phi:v1:L3rRU8SD…"` where `"Assigned note"` is expected, and staff viewing *another provider's* booking get `"phi:v1:R/aPIZYl…"` where `null` (masked) is expected. So the decrypt/mask step is not running on this read path and the stored value passes through untouched. **This is not a plaintext leak** — the data is encrypted at rest and stays encrypted — but authorised staff see gibberish instead of notes, and unauthorised staff receive ciphertext rather than nothing, which still discloses that notes exist and is a leak outright if the caller can decrypt. The spec does supply `PHI_ENCRYPTION_MASTER_KEY` via a mocked ConfigService, so a missing key is not the explanation; suspect the read path stopped calling the PHI codec, or reads a renamed config key. Sprint 37 tests, failing now and unnoticed because CI is red on every push (`e2e-bug.449`). **— That entire diagnosis was wrong, and the "serious" framing with it. Corrected and closed §192.** The read path is fine. `findOne` calls `decryptBookingForStaff`, but only when `resolveStaffPhiContext` returns a context, and that returns `null` unless `isStaffMemberUserId(userId)` passes — a **strict UUID test** added with `e2e-bug.97` to stop guest `customer:<uuid>` actors querying BusinessMember. The specs passed the synthetic id `'staff-1'`, which is not a UUID, so PHI processing was skipped entirely and the stored value passed through as ciphertext. **`User.id` is `@PrimaryGeneratedColumn('uuid')`, so real staff ids always pass the guard and production was never affected.** The tell was in the same file: it *already* asserts `'staff-not-a-uuid'` → `null`/`public`, so the guard was known and deliberately tested — the happy-path fixtures were simply never updated when it landed. **Fix: four fixture strings**, `'staff-1'` → a valid UUID; both suites now 9/9. The two deliberate non-UUID cases are untouched. **Lesson: I inferred a production defect from a failing assertion without tracing why it failed** — the ciphertext in the output was evidence that a code path had been skipped, not that it had misbehaved |
| `e2e-bug.473` | **Mostly fixed 2026-08-19 (§214)** | no | **Test debris is committed and running.** `backend/src/modules/ai/zzz-e2.` is a **tracked 0-byte file with a truncated name** (a shell redirect that lost its extension); `backend/src/modules/ai/zzz-a4-probe.spec.ts` is a tracked 1.5 KB probe; `backend/src/modules/ai/eval/zz-state.spec.ts` is an **untracked** 939-byte diagnostic whose only assertion is `expect(r.failed).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(0)` — it always passes and exists to `console.log` corpus state, so it runs and prints on every `npm test`. Jest's cache also still references a deleted `src/modules/ai/zz-t0.spec.ts`, producing a spurious "Test suite failed to run — ENOENT" in the full run. Left in place rather than deleted: two are tracked (someone committed them deliberately or accidentally, and that is theirs to decide) and the untracked one is unrecoverable if removed. Found in §191 — and one was mine: `zz-scratch-loc.spec.ts` survived the §189 cleanup and **has been removed**.
  **§214 cleared the two tracked ones, and the probe turned out to matter more than "debris" suggested.** `zzz-a4-probe.spec.ts` opens a real Postgres connection and reads `ai_command_trace`, so it **can never pass in CI** — yet it held a slot in `ai-known-failures.json` as an *accepted* failure. The manifest's rule is that entries leave only by being fixed; an entry that is unfixable by construction is a **permanent occupant**, which is precisely the dumping ground that rule exists to prevent. Renamed to `zzz-a4-probe.probe.ts` rather than deleted — `testRegex` is `.*\.spec\.ts$`, so jest stops collecting it while the tool survives for whoever wants to run it by hand, with a header explaining why it is not a `.spec.ts` and how to run it. History preserved via `git mv`. The 0-byte `zzz-e2.` was `git rm`'d (recoverable from history if it ever meant something). Manifest **65/202 → 64/201**. **Left alone deliberately:** `eval/zz-state.spec.ts` is **untracked**, so deleting it is unrecoverable — it always passes and only prints corpus diagnostics, so it costs noise on `npm test` and nothing else. That one is the owner's call |
| `e2e-bug.474` | **Re-scoped 2026-08-28 (§215) — not a flaky suite; an observation-conditions problem** | no | **The known-failures sweep compares failure sets exactly, so one flaky test turns it red.** Measured 2026-08-17 while wiring the gate into `ci.yml` (§191): the sweep passed standalone twice — **316 s** idle and **1025 s** on a loaded machine, both `71 suites · 203 tests ✓ matches manifest exactly` — but **exited 1** when run back-to-back immediately after `test:ai-roadmap-gates`. Nothing in the code changed between those runs, so the difference is load: under contention a timing-sensitive suite fails, the failure set no longer matches the manifest, and the gate reports red. A separate transient was seen the same day in the gate chain itself (one run reported a failing suite; two immediately after reported none). **Do not "fix" this by loosening the comparison** — exactness is what makes the gate catch both new breakage and silent fixes. Options, in order of preference: identify and stabilise the flaky suite(s); give the sweep its own CI job so it never contends with the gate chain on one runner; or retry once on failure. Also note the 3× spread in wall-clock makes `timeout-minutes` guesswork — currently 45. **Mitigated 2026-08-20 with option 2, not option 1.** The sweep now has its own `ai-known-failures` job in `ci.yml`, so it never shares a runner with the gate chain — removing the exact contention that produced the one observed red (it passed standalone twice and failed only when chained). It also runs *beside* the gates rather than after them, so the job wall-clock is max(gates, sweep) rather than the sum. **This does not stabilise the flaky suite**, which remains the preferred fix and is still open: a timing-sensitive test can still flip the set on a slow runner, just without the gate chain's load on top. Identifying it needs repeated runs, not reasoning — the failure was never reproduced deterministically.
  **Partly misdiagnosed — corrected §195.** At least one "transient" reading was **a stale jest cache, not load**. The gate chain reported `ai-declared-inputs.rule` at 54/54 in a captured run, while `ai-command-spec.remaining.ts` had been modified *before* that run and the suite fails **deterministically** (3/3 runs) once the cache turns over. Corroborating evidence from the same session: a full `npm test` reported `Test suite failed to run — ENOENT … zz-t0.spec.ts` for a file that no longer exists, which is only possible from cached state. So a green gate here can be a **false green**, which is worse than the false red this ticket was filed about. The sweep's load sensitivity may still be real, but it is no longer the only explanation and should not be assumed. Before trusting a gate result after editing registry inputs, clear the cache (`npx jest --clearCache`) or verify the specific suite directly.
  **Fourth data point, 2026-08-29 (§226): deterministic when quiet, variable under load — and the gate defends itself.** §215 measured two identical full runs with a **zero-difference** failure set and retired "one timing-sensitive test flips it routinely". Under `--update`'s own sweep, one suite did flip: `ai-detector-retirement.boundary › Phase 8 retirement readiness still has the paraphrase detectors it started with`. Run on its own it is **18/18**. So the refined statement is: the failure set is stable on a quiet machine and can vary under contention, which is why the 3× wall-clock spread was the right signal all along. **What matters more is that the tooling already handles this correctly**: `--update` **refused to write**, naming the entry and saying *"Most often these are flakes under load, not regressions — run the named suites on their own first"*, and requiring `--allow-new` for a genuine addition. A retry on a quieter machine wrote cleanly. That is the manifest's shrink-only rule doing its job — a flake cannot silently become an accepted failure |
  **Both directions now confirmed (§198).** A sweep run reported `72 failing suites · 205 failing tests · manifest 203` with `✗ 2 failure(s) NOT in the manifest — this change broke them`, naming both `ai-declared-inputs.rule` T0 ceiling tests. Re-run on the **identical tree** with no code change in between (only markdown edits): `71 · 203 · ✓ matches the manifest exactly`. The same suite passed 54/54 standalone and again in an 11-suite parallel run, both times. So this gate produces **false reds as well as false greens**, on an unchanged tree — a change author would reasonably have concluded they broke the ceiling and "fixed" a number that was already correct. The 3× wall-clock spread (316 s / 796 s / 1025 s across runs) is the strongest available signal that the cause is contention rather than logic.
  **Measured properly on a quiet tree, 2026-08-28 (§215): the failure set is deterministic.** Two identical back-to-back full runs — `jest --testPathPatterns=src/`, 1,942 suites, 40,157 tests each — produced **44 failing suites / 84 failing tests both times, with a zero-difference failure set** (0 failed-in-A-not-B, 0 failed-in-B-not-A, compared by `suite :: fullName`). **That disproves the hypothesis this ticket was filed on** — "exact-set matching means a single timing-sensitive test flips this red" — at least as a routine effect. What the false red (§198) and false green (§195) have in common is not timing but **conditions**: every one was observed while the working tree was being edited by a concurrent session, or against a jest cache that predated an edit. So this is not a flaky test suite; it is a gate that was being read while its input was moving. **Practical consequence, and it is cheaper than chasing a flake:** do not trust a sweep verdict taken while anything is writing to the tree, and clear the cache after editing registry inputs. **Caveat kept deliberately:** two runs on a quiet tree cannot prove the absence of a rare flake, and this says nothing about a loaded CI runner — but it does retire "one timing-sensitive test flips it routinely" as the working explanation |
| `e2e-bug.475` | **Open — needs a product answer** | no | **Should a tour checkout require prepayment even when the service says `prepaymentMode: NONE`?** `booking-payment.tour.spec.ts` asserts `prepaymentAmount: 340` (85 × 4 pax) and `prepaymentAmount: 85` for the single-traveller case, while its own `tourService` fixture declares `prepaymentMode: PrepaymentMode.NONE`. Production returns **0**, and the 0 does not come from a stubbed repo: `sumServicesPrepaymentAmount` short-circuits on `if (!serviceIds.length) return 0` before touching `serviceRepo`, so the checkout is not putting the tour service into `serviceIds` at all. `servicePrice` is correct (340), so the per-person multiplication works — only the prepayment half disagrees. Two readings, and they need different fixes: either **tours are meant to prepay in full** regardless of mode (production bug — the tour service never reaches the prepayment sum), or **the test encodes an intention that was never implemented** and `NONE` really does mean zero (stale expectation). Found in §196 while clearing the mechanical class of `e2e-bug.471`; deliberately not guessed, because the two fixes point in opposite directions and one of them changes what customers are charged |
| `e2e-bug.484` | **Closed 2026-08-19 (§216) — my diagnosis was wrong; the roster is populated** | no | **`ClassifierCatalog.locations` is read but never written, so `e2e-bug.460`'s location-vs-employee disambiguation cannot engage on the dashboard.** `buildDashboardUnderstandInput` (`dashboard-command-understanding.adapter.ts:68`) destructures `locations` off the catalog and forwards `locations ?? []` into the pipeline, but **no code path anywhere assigns it**: grepping `locations` across the AI module finds only the type declarations (`command-completion.types.ts:23`, the rescue service's own parameters), the pipeline pass-through (`command-understanding-pipeline.service.ts:676`, `locations: input.locations`), and this read. Every `catalog: {…}` producer in `ai-command.service.ts` (5 sites: 1054, 3108, 3375, 3743, 3894) declares the inline shape `{ employees; services; customers; templates }` with no `locations`, and the single production call site (`ai-command.service.ts:1129`) passes that object straight through. So the dashboard surface always resolves with an **empty location roster**, and the §189 fix — which was verified working when a roster is supplied directly to `tryRescueStaffOperations` (`Downtown` → `update_location`, `Maria` → `update_employee`) — is inert on the surface it was written for. Note the fix's own verification went through the rescue service with a roster passed by hand, which is exactly why the gap survived: **the probe supplied the input that production does not.** Found in §197 because the missing field was also a build error (the adapter read a property the type did not declare, so `nest build` failed and the backend would not start); adding `locations?:` to `ClassifierCatalog` fixed the build but not the behaviour. Fix is to populate the roster where the catalog is built — the same place `employees` comes from — and then re-verify through the dashboard entry point rather than through the rescue helper. Related: `e2e-bug.470` (the eval's roster fixtures are per-surface and cannot measure this today). **— That diagnosis was wrong, and the ticket is closed §216.** `executeCommand` loads the roster (`ai-command.service.ts:587`, `this.locations.listLocations(businessId)`, deliberately joined to the existing parallel batch) and puts it on the catalog it builds; `BusinessCatalog` has declared `locations?` all along, with a comment naming `e2e-bug.460`; and the branch-scope path preserves it because `applyBranchScopeToCatalogLogic` returns `{ ...catalog, employees }`. The object the dashboard adapter reads is that same object, so the disambiguation gets a real roster. **What I actually found was a type gap, not a behaviour gap:** five intermediate `catalog: { employees; services; customers; templates }` parameter types in `ai-command.service.ts` omitted `locations`, so the field survived only by structural pass-through and was invisible to the compiler — which is why the adapter's read did not typecheck and the build broke in §197. **Root cause of the wrong call:** I grepped `catalog: {` and got those five *parameter type declarations*, then read them as producers. I never grepped `const catalog = {`, which is the only line that answers 'who writes this'. A type declaration cannot populate a field, and I treated five of them as proof that nothing did. **Fixed in §216:** the five parameter types now use the shared `BusinessCatalog`, so a future hop that rebuilds the catalog and drops the roster fails the compiler instead of silently degrading; two regression tests in `dashboard-command-understanding.adapter.spec.ts` pin both halves of the contract (roster forwarded when present, `[]` when the producer omits it) |
| `e2e-bug.483` | **Open — hardening** | no | **Double-booking is prevented entirely in application code; the database would accept two overlapping bookings without complaint.** Not a live defect — `api-bug.4`'s fix is correct and was verified live 7/7 under 4-way and 8-way races. This is about the *blast radius* when something eventually bypasses it. `BookingService.create` ([`booking.service.ts:387`](./backend/src/modules/booking/booking.service.ts)) does it properly: one `dataSource.transaction`, `claimSlotsInWindowForUpdate` (`:2305`) taking `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` over the window's micro-slots ordered by `(startTime, id)` so concurrent claims acquire row locks in a consistent sequence (deadlock avoidance), a hard reject when slots exist but none are claimable (`e2e-bug.119`), then a second `FOR UPDATE` over overlapping non-cancelled `Booking` rows (`:403`) → `ConflictException`. **But there is no constraint underneath any of it.** Checked all 58 files in `database/migrations/`: `bookings` carries indexes only (`booking.entity.ts:34`, `['employeeId','startTime','endTime']`) — no `UNIQUE`, no `EXCLUDE`, and no trigger. Other tables do use `UNIQUE` (`clinic-after-visit-summaries`, `patient-encounters`, `sprint5-resources-subscriptions`), so the omission is specific to the one table where the race actually lives. Every guarantee therefore depends on all writers routing through that one method, and the tree already has several booking-creation entry points (`scheduling-engine.service.ts:283`, `workflow-step-executors.service.ts:151`, plus the public/dashboard/provider paths); any future admin action, import script or backfill that inserts a `Booking` directly gets no protection and fails silently rather than loudly. **The obvious backstop does not fit this domain as written, and that is the substance of the ticket.** The textbook form — `CREATE EXTENSION btree_gist; ALTER TABLE bookings ADD CONSTRAINT bookings_no_overlap EXCLUDE USING gist (employee_id WITH =, tstzrange(start_time, end_time) WITH &&) WHERE (status <> 'cancelled')` — **would reject valid bookings**, for two independent reasons: (1) `maxAppointmentCount` defaults to 1 but is a configurable column (`scheduling-slot.entity.ts:72`, also on `scheduling-period` and `scheduling-template-period`), so group classes legitimately put several bookings on one provider at one instant; (2) multi-service visits deliberately allow overlapping same-employee segments — the conflict query explicitly excludes rows sharing a `multiServiceGroupId`. Worse, the capacity that decides legality lives on the **slot** row, not the booking row, so it is not expressible in a constraint over `bookings` at all. Workable options, in rough order of cost: a **narrowed partial constraint** covering the capacity-1 single-service case (`WHERE status <> 'cancelled' AND multi_service_group_id IS NULL`), which catches the common shape but silently exempts exactly the rows that are hardest to reason about; or a **trigger** that joins to the slot row and enforces against `maxAppointmentCount`, which is complete but puts booking policy in PL/pgSQL. Note also `config/database.config.ts:12` sets `synchronize: NODE_ENV !== 'production'`, so a constraint needs both an entity change and a migration or dev and prod schemas diverge. Raised from a design question rather than an observed failure; **not implemented, because narrowing it wrongly would start rejecting real bookings** and the choice between the two options is a product call about how much of the capacity model belongs in the database |
| `e2e-bug.476` | **Fixed 2026-08-18 (§198 routing, §199 schedule hint)** | yes | **"who offers Deep tissue massage usally?" answered "There are currently no providers available for Deep tissue massage on 19 August 2026."** Two defects, one fixed. **(1) Routing — FIXED.** The prompt has no day in it ("usually" is the opposite of one), yet it was answered as a dated availability query. `isLookupServiceAssignmentPrompt` — the predicate for the command whose whole job is "who is assigned to a service" — matched **1 of 8** natural capability phrasings, including the command spec's own documented example **`'who does facials'`** and its documented classifier trigger `"who is doing {service}"`. Its vocabulary (`doing\|performing\|giving\|working\|assigned\|scheduled`) knows availability words and provider nouns but **no capability verb**: `offers` appears in no predicate anywhere in the AI module. Its own third clause (`which providers are assigned`) was **dead code**, because `isCheckProvidersForServicePrompt` claims anything containing "which…providers" on the word `providers` and is tested first. Fixed by a tight `isPureCapabilityQuestion` — a capability verb directly after who/which, and **no availability word and no day** — checked before the availability predicate. Corpus **8463/8464, +0%**; 11 phrasings pinned in `ai-intent-disambiguation.util.spec.ts`. **(2) The schedule hint — NOT fixed.** The user also expected "…but has no schedule for tomorrow, create one". `lookup_service_assignment` now correctly answers `Providers who can perform Deep tissue massage (1): • Gevorg`, which names the provider, but it is a pure catalog answer and says nothing about whether that provider has a schedule, nor offers to create one. **Delivered in §199.** The capability answer now appends: `No Deep tissue massage time is scheduled for <day> — add one with "create a schedule for Gevorg".`, and `details.unscheduledProviders` / `details.unscheduledOn` carry the same fact structurally so a caller can offer the action without parsing prose. Implemented as **one batched `periodRepo` query** for the whole roster rather than one per provider (the existing `getProviderAvailabilityForService` costs ~3 queries each and also computes open slots, which an un-dated answer does not show). The addendum is **non-fatal by construction** — if the schedule read throws, the catalog answer still returns, because it is complete without it. Four cases pinned including the single-query assertion and the degradation path. **Note the first measurement of the fix cost 2 cases**: `speciali[sz]es in` reads like a capability verb but is `explain_provider_specialty`'s trigger — removed, and recorded in the code so it is not re-added |

Traced mechanically; all three paths converge on the same edge: `ai-flexible-availability.util → ai-structural-extractors → booking-first-available.semantic.util → intent-phrasing-bank.util → intent-anchor.seed.util → **ai-prompt-i18n → ai-service-discovery-multilingual.fixtures**`. **The root cause is a layering violation:** `ai-prompt-i18n.ts` is production (imported by `ai-booking-core.service.ts`, `ai-prompt-normalization.service.ts`, `customer-ai-command.util.ts`) and it imports **dozens of `*.fixtures.ts` modules** for classifier-rule text. **Fixed narrowly:** `ai-prompt-i18n` needed only `SERVICE_DISCOVERY_MULTILINGUAL_CLASSIFIER_RULES`, a self-contained template string, so it moved to `ai-service-discovery-multilingual.rules.ts` (zero imports) with the fixtures module re-exporting it. Production no longer pulls the scenario-building graph. **The broader violation stands** — production importing fixtures for classifier rules is the shape that made this possible, and the same cycle can close again through any of the other fixture modules `ai-prompt-i18n` imports |
| `e2e-bug.478` | **Fixed 2026-08-18 (§204)** | yes | **"Do I pay online for this service?" routes to `pay_online` (T2, "Start or continue a card checkout for a booking") instead of `explain_payment_options_for_service`.** A yes/no question about payment policy resolves to a checkout-initiating command, on the **public/guest** surface. Found in §201: the assertion has existed all along in `public-booking-assistant.pipeline.integration.spec.ts:93` but had never executed, because `e2e-bug.477`'s import cycle stopped the suite loading — so this is newly *visible*, not newly broken, and there is no way to tell how long it has been wrong. It had `can i`, `could i` and `do you` — but not **`do i`**. So "Do I pay online…" fell through as an explicit instruction to pay. Added `do i`, `must i`, `should i`; all three are listed as triggers for `explain_payment_options_for_service` in **its own classifier rules** ("do I pay online for color", "must I pay online for the selected service"), so the gap was between the documented contract and the predicate rather than a new judgement. Corpus **8463/8464, +0%**; instructions still route to checkout (`pay online`, `pay online now`, `pay with my card`, `continue to checkout` all still true), 9 phrasings pinned both directions; assistant suites **106/106**, payment suites **121/121** |
| `e2e-bug.479` | **Fixed 2026-08-18 (§203)** | no | **A clinic task test was anchored to the real clock, so it passed in June 2026 and rotted.** `clinic-task-auto.util.spec.ts` asserted `draft.dueAt` equals `resolveResultReviewDueAt(completedAt, completedAt)` — pinning the clock on the *expectation* side — while letting the builder fall through to `new Date()`. `resolveResultReviewDueAt` deliberately clamps a due date that is already past **up to `now`** (`return dueAt.getTime() > now.getTime() ? dueAt : now`), so once real time passed `completedAt + CLINIC_RESULT_REVIEW_DUE_HOURS` the builder returned *today* and the assertion could never hold again. **Production is correct** — a result completed months ago should not be given a due date in the past — and the builder already accepted `now?: Date`; the test simply never passed it. Fixed by pinning `now: completedAt`. **Checked the four sibling builder calls in the same file: none pass `now` either, but all four are safe** — `resolveSpecimenCollectionDueAt` returns `bookingStartTime ?? specimenCreatedAt` and `resolveLabBookingRequestCallbackDueAt` is a pure function of `pushedAt`, so neither reads the clock. The result-review clamp was the only time-dependent path, which is why it was the only failure |
| `e2e-bug.480` | **Fixed 2026-08-18 (§207)** | yes | **`queryMentionsName` rejects an Armenian name the moment it takes a grammatical ending, so "գրանցեք ինձ Աննայի մոտ" does not resolve Աննա.** The boundary test is Unicode-aware (`/[\p{L}\p{N}]/u`, not `\b`) and correctly refuses a name buried inside a word — but Armenian is agglutinative and attaches case suffixes directly, so the genitive `Աննայի` is `Աննա` + `յի` and the character after the name is a letter. Measured in §206 while migrating the three `fuzzyMatchByName` copies: the old raw `includes` tier matched it *by accident*, so the three copies happened to handle Armenian inflection while the canonical does not. **Russian is not affected the way `D5-d` recorded** — «запишите меня к Марии» fails in **both** implementations, because the inflected form is not a superstring of the nominative. So the caveat is narrower and more specific than "drops inflected hy/ru names": it is Armenian suffixation only, and it applies to the **canonical** matcher, i.e. it was already live on every path that used it. **Not a regression introduced by §206** — that slice made three surfaces consistent with behaviour already shipped elsewhere. **Fixed in §207 with a closed set of case endings, not a length allowance.** Measured first, because the obvious fix is the wrong one: a blanket "allow up to three trailing letters" matches **Անի inside անիծիր** ("curse") and **Արա inside արագ** ("quick") — e2e-bug.362 reproduced in another script. `ARMENIAN_CASE_SUFFIX` accepts only real endings (`յին|յից|յով|ներին|ների|յի|ին|ից|ով|ուն|ու|ը|ն|ի`), the ending must itself end the word, and the allowance only applies when the *name* contains Armenian characters — so Latin matching is untouched and e2e-bug.362 stays fixed. **Russian is explicitly out of scope and documented as such**: «Мария» → «Марии» is a stem change, not a suffix, so no rule of this shape reaches it; it needs stemming. Corpus **8463/8464, +0%**; 11 cases pinned in `ai-orchestration.helpers.spec.ts` covering the inflections, both false-positive traps, the Latin e2e-bug.362 cases, and the Russian gap recorded as a known miss |
| `e2e-bug.481` | **Closed 2026-08-20** | no | **«Иван» → «Ивана» / «Ивану» does not resolve, and unlike the feminine case it is reachable.** §207 closed the Armenian half of `e2e-bug.447`'s regression with a closed set of case endings, and its docblock claimed Russian was out of scope because «Мария» → «Марии» is a stem change. **That is true for the feminine forms and wrong for the masculine ones**: `fuzzy-match-copies.boundary.spec.ts` pins «запиши Ивана» and «к Ивану», which are the name plus a clean one-letter suffix — exactly the shape `ARMENIAN_CASE_SUFFIX` handles. Corrected in the docblock in §208. **Why it was not simply added:** Russian case endings are largely **single letters** (`-а -у -е -ы -ом -ой`), so an allowlist collides with ordinary words far more readily than the Armenian multi-character set — the same false-positive risk that made a length-based rule unusable in §207 (`Անի` inside `անիծիր`). Needs its own corpus score and probably a requirement that the ending be preceded by a name of some minimum length. The feminine stem-change forms («Марии») remain out of reach without stemming **Fixed:** `RUSSIAN_CASE_SUFFIX` in `ai-orchestration.helpers.ts` — closed set `ом|ем|а|у|е`, ending must end the word, gated on Cyrillic and a 3-character minimum stem (Armenian needs no such guard; Russian endings are single letters and collide far more readily). Feminine stem change («Мария»→«Марии») is out of scope by construction and pinned as an explicit known-miss test |
  **Measured 2026-08-18 (§209) and the blocker moved: the fix is not the hard part, measuring it is.** Two findings. **(1) The collision risk is worse than estimated.** A single-letter allowance makes `Мир` match «мира» ("of peace") and `Дом` match «дома» — the §207 trap in another script, and this time the endings are one character so the allowlist cannot discriminate. **(2) The corpus cannot score it.** Every Russian inflected name in the fixtures is **feminine with a stem change** — `Марии` ×52, `Анны` ×8, `Анне` ×6, `Анну`, `Марию` — i.e. `Мария`→`Марии` and `Анна`→`Анну` replace the final vowel rather than appending, so **no suffix rule reaches a single one of them**. There is **not one masculine inflected Russian name** (`Ивана`/`Ивану`/`Иваном`) anywhere in the corpus. So the tractable half is unmeasurable and the measurable half is intractable. Shipping the masculine rule would be an unscored change — the exact "untestable, not disproved" state §188 was written about. **Prerequisite: add masculine-inflection cases to the corpus** (related to `e2e-bug.470` — a fixture set that cannot exercise the rule cannot gate it), then score. Note the 52 `Марии` prompts are `tx(hy, ru)` **compound-decomposition** pairs, so they do not exercise `fuzzyMatchByName` at all — their Armenian twins (`Մարիայի`, `Աննայի`, `Ջոնի`) do carry the genitive endings §207 now handles |

Materialization fills *future* days only, so the test passes exactly while a matching weekday falls strictly inside the window. Jul 29 + 21 days = **2026-08-19**, which was future on 18 Aug (pass) and is "today" on 19 Aug (excluded → `savedPeriods.length` is 0). The fix landed as this entry recommended — the window is now relative (`isoDay(dayOffset(1))`) rather than the absolute `2026-07-31 → 2026-08-20`, and the spec carries a comment naming this bug. **Checked that it cannot recur rather than only that it passes today:** every absolute date left in the three files (`2026-08-01`, `2026-08-07`) is already in the **past**, and a past absolute date is stable — it cannot expire further — whereas a future one has a deadline. That distinction is the actual rule, and it is what makes these three safe |

| `e2e-bug.491` | **Fixed 2026-08-21** | yes | **Two package-visit specs failed only within ~2 hours of midnight UTC, turning the shared known-failures gate red for nobody's change.** `public-customer-booking.integration.spec.ts` and `public-customer-booking.service.spec.ts` both build `futureStart = Date.now() + 72h`, then lay package lines an hour apart from `futureStart + 24h`. That block inherits the **current time of day**, so a run at 23:55 UTC put the second line on the next day and `reschedulePackageVisitInternal` rejected it with *"All package services must be scheduled on the same day"* — 7 failures across the two files. Computed, not guessed: `blockStart 2026-08-24T23:55` → `line +60m → 2026-08-25T00:55`. **Fixed** by anchoring `futureStart` with `setUTCHours(10,0,0,0)` in both — the date stays genuinely in the future, only the within-day arithmetic becomes deterministic. Verified clock-independent by simulating all **24 launch hours**: same-day at every one; 70/70 tests pass; `npm run test:ai-known-failures` back to exit 0, *"Failure set matches the manifest exactly"*. **Swept for the class**, not just the instance: 41 specs derive times from `Date.now()`, 5 also involve same-day/sequential logic, and the other 3 were checked and are **not** vulnerable — they have no same-day constraint, so they were deliberately left alone rather than speculatively "fixed". Same class as e2e-bug.482 (tests that expire on a calendar) and a live instance of e2e-bug.474's concern (one flake reddens an exact-match gate). **Follow-up 2026-08-21 — the cure has its own trap, found the hard way.** Converting a hardcoded date to `new Date(Date.now() + N).toISOString()` is safe only if the value is used **once**. `ai-reschedule-package-visit-self.logic.spec.ts` used it for both a test's *input* and its *expected* `toHaveBeenCalledWith` value; two calls land on different milliseconds whenever the clock ticks between them, so the strings differed in their last digits and the test failed about **one run in three**. The literal it replaced was identical in both places by construction — so making it relative *introduced* a flake while removing an expiry. **Rule:** freeze the value once (a module-level helper over a single `Date.now()`), or anchor the hour so seconds and milliseconds are zeroed. Verified 5 consecutive clean runs after the fix; the sibling `lines` spec was already safe because its `futureAt` anchors the hour, and the `cancel` spec because it asserts on ids, not timestamps. **A second class-member found 2026-08-23, and it changes how to hunt for these.** `ai-tour-calendar-week.logic` (3 manifest entries) is date-dependent too, but its symptom is an **assertion** (`departureCount` expected 2, got 0), not a crash: `handleListTourCalendarWeekLogic` falls back to `getTodayDateKey()` when it cannot read a week anchor, so fixtures hardcoded to 2026-06-11/12 fall outside the current week and nothing matches. **This means a TypeError-based triage cannot find expired fixtures** — the earlier pass that classified manifest suites as "mechanical vs assertion" would have filed every one of these as an assertion mismatch. Hunt them by *date literal*, not by error class. That hunt found **17 manifest suites / 31 tests** still carrying past date literals, and the first one worked through — `customer-ai-command-reschedule-my-booking.integration` (5 tests) — was exactly this class: `buildRescheduleOwnedBookingMatchParams` matches only *upcoming* visits, so once today passed 2026-08-18 both fixtures fell out of range and the id list came back empty. Made relative, preserving the original 20-day gap and their order: **5/5 pass**, verified clock-independent at all 24 launch hours; manifest 101 → 96. A past literal is a *candidate*, not proof — e2e-bug.492 found many are pinned-clock and legitimate — so each still needs its own check. **Attempted and reverted**: anchoring that spec's week to `Date.now()` took it from 3 failures to 4, because the logic snaps the anchor to a week boundary and my offsets did not match that snapping. Reverted cleanly (no diff); the fix needs the real week-normalisation, not naive day offsets. **Swept the class, and it is a single instance.** Five other specs match structurally (>=2 inline `Date.now() + …toISOString()` expressions *and* an equality assertion) — `public-booking-cash.integration` (9), `ai-cancel-package-visit-self.util`, `ai-reschedule-package-visit-self.util`, `booking-payment.service`, `ai-cancel-package-visit-self.logic`. **All stable over three consecutive runs each**, because a structural match is not the defect: the race needs the *same logical value* computed twice *and compared*, which only the one spec did. Left unchanged rather than speculatively "fixed" — recorded so the sweep is not repeated |
| `e2e-bug.492` | **Closed 2026-08-21 — audit, no defect found** | no | **Audited every spec with a hardcoded future date, after e2e-bug.482 and e2e-bug.491 made "tests that expire" look systemic. It is not.** Swept `backend/src` for absolute `YYYY-MM-DD` literals in specs and classified each against today. Two safe shapes cover essentially all of them: **far-future semantics** (gift-card expiry `2028-01-01`, sentinels `2099-01-01`) where pastness is irrelevant for years, and **a pinned clock** — `ai-orchestration.helpers.spec.ts` asserts "revenue this month" → `2026-08-01…2026-08-31`, which would expire on 1 Sep, except the suite calls `jest.useFakeTimers().setSystemTime('2026-08-15T12:00:00.000Z')` first, making it deterministic forever. **That is the correct pattern and the direct counterexample to e2e-bug.491**, which failed precisely because it took the time of day from a live clock. So the rule the two bugs suggest is narrower than "absolute dates are dangerous": **a spec may use an absolute date if it pins the clock, and may use a live clock only for "later than now" — never for the current time of day, and never for a future absolute window it does not control.** Recorded as a closed audit so the sweep is not repeated; the one oddity noted in passing — `booking-slot-resolver.service.spec.ts` contains `2026-19-08` (month 19) — was **checked and is deliberate**: e2e-bug.343's fixture for a DD/MM-vs-MM/DD misinterpretation, asserting the resolver degrades to a clarification instead of crashing. Nothing to fix; recorded so the next reader does not re-flag it |

Third instance of the `e2e-bug.494` class in one pass (`ServiceService.create`/`update`, `CommandResult.sessionContext`, this) — **but with a different cause worth knowing: a conditional spread also defeats inference**, so unlike `create`/`update` this one could not have been fixed by deleting the annotation; the field has to be declared. **Fix:** added `remainingSpots?: number` with the reason inline. **−4 errors, no offset**; the 3 failing tests in the two tour suites are pre-existing and match the manifest exactly (2 + 1) |
