The post-gate's six real uses sit at **five points inside** `runRescueClassifiedPhase`/`runRescueUnknownPhase` plus one helper — methods that each contain hundreds of `if (x) return x` sites. So coverage is roughly **5% of return sites**, and the ticket's concern is confirmed rather than merely suspected. **But there is still no measured instance:** the 8,464-case corpus shows zero (see e2e-bug.435), so this stays hardening. **Recommended shape, because the obvious one does not work:** you cannot gate at each `return x` — returning the gate's `null` exits the phase and blocks the chain, which is the original bug. Each site would need `const g = gate(x); if (g) return g;`, i.e. **356 edits**. A **lazy generator** per phase — yield candidates, let one central consumer take the first surface-legal one — gets central gating without losing the chain's laziness, in one place instead of 356 |
| `e2e-bug.498` | **Fixed 2026-08-21** | yes | **`npm run test:ai-known-failures:update` absorbs flaky suites into the manifest, permanently widening the ratchet.** Run on 2026-08-21 to record 47 genuine fixes, it removed those 47 **and silently added 11 unrelated suites** — `business-currency`, `business-tax`, `public-booking-compliance`, `stripe-currency` and seven siblings — each as a bare `*` (whole-suite failure). **All of them pass in isolation** (spot-checked three: 72, then 39 tests, all green), so they were transients under load, not regressions. The update mode regenerates the manifest from one sweep with no notion of "only shrink", so any suite that flakes during that sweep becomes an *accepted* failure forever — the opposite of what the ratchet is for, and undetectable afterwards because the manifest then matches. This is `e2e-bug.474`'s flake risk with a worse ending: 474 says a flake turns the gate red, which is noisy but safe; this makes it **green and wrong**. **Worked around by hand this time** — the manifest was rebuilt as *before minus the 47 verified fixes*, never plus anything. **Fix:** make `--update` removal-only (drop entries that now pass, refuse to add), or require `--force` to add, so recording a fix cannot quietly widen the accepted set | **Fixed: `--update` is now removal-only.** `scripts/ai-known-failures-gate.mjs` diffs the fresh sweep against the existing manifest and **refuses** if any entry would be added, printing the offending entries and pointing at the likely cause (*"most often these are flakes under load, not regressions — run the named suites on their own first"*). Adding requires an explicit `--allow-new`, which is the "deliberate act that needs a reason and a ticket" the manifest's own `$comment` always claimed. **The code now enforces the contract it documented.** Verified by dropping one manifest entry and re-running: *"--update refused: it would ADD 2 entr(ies)… the manifest may only shrink"*. The 47 genuine fixes were recorded by hand in the meantime (63 → 58 suites, 200 → 153 tests), removals only |
| `e2e-bug.499` | **Open — 120 remaining; two clusters proved to be defects, not decisions** | no | **The known-failures manifest was a third stale, and what remains is a different kind of problem.** Cleared **67 tests across 10 suites** on 2026-08-21, in two mechanical classes, neither of which was disputed behaviour: **(a) expired fixtures** — 3 suites whose bookings were hardcoded to dates now past, so `startTime >= now` matched nothing (e2e-bug.491's class); **(b) `mock_missing_transaction`** — 7 suites, 48 tests, all one root cause: `ensureBookingManageToken` opens `bookingRepo.manager.transaction` and locks the row inside it, and none of those mocks had a `manager`, so every case threw before reaching the behaviour under test. Manifest **200 → 133 tests, 63 → 53 suites**. **The mechanical seam is now mined out** — every remaining suite was re-run and classified, and all are `assertion_mismatch`: the code and the test disagree, which the manifest's own `reasons` says *"needs a product decision (is the test stale, or did the behaviour regress?)"*. Largest: `ai-service-discovery.integration` (22, e.g. expects `haircut` and gets `cut`), `ai-implication-corpus.fixtures` (9), `ai-service-discovery.eval` (8), `ai-e2e248-reschedule-nearest.util` (7). **Recorded so the triage is not redone:** "200 known failures" was never 200 open questions. **Now 101, and the mechanical seam is confirmed exhausted** — a second classification pass on 2026-08-23 ran nine more manifest suites individually (`ai-flexible-availability.integration`, `ai-tour-calendar-week.logic`, `dashboard-revenue-analytics.integration`, `ai-customer-deferred-locale-parity`, `customer-ai-command-reschedule-my-booking.integration`, `fast-intent-heuristics.boundary`, `ai-structural-extractors`, `booking-payment.tour`, `ai-booking-depth.integration`) and **every one is assertion-level**; no `TypeError`/missing-mock failures remain anywhere in the manifest. **But "assertion-level" has not meant "product decision"** — of the clusters actually opened, four were defects (rescue shadowing, `delete serviceRank`, and the two already fixed) and one is a documented fixture contradiction (e2e-bug.501). The label groups by error *shape*, not cause, and reading it as "needs product input" is what kept these unexamined. **Started on the largest cluster and ruled out the obvious fix.** `ai-service-discovery.integration`'s failures are one disagreement repeated: for *"Best rated stylist for a **cut** under $60"* the classifier supplies `serviceCategory: 'haircut'`, and `enrichDiscoveryParamsFromPrompt` overwrites it with `'cut'` — `if (category) { rankedParams.serviceCategory = category; }` in `ai-service-discovery-enrichment.util.ts`, an unconditional assignment. Two facts make the code look wrong rather than the tests: `'cut'` appears **nowhere** in the fixtures' category vocabulary (`haircut`, `massage`, `facial`, `styling`, `manicure`, `hair`), and the same fixture's `catalogParams` expect `'haircut'` downstream, so `'cut'` would filter the catalog wrongly. **But the obvious fix does not work:** changing the assignment to gap-fill (`if (category && !rankedParams.serviceCategory)`) leaves the failure count **unchanged at 30** — measured, then reverted. So the answer is not "stop overwriting"; it is a question about what `extractServiceRankServiceCategoryFromPrompt` should return for a bare *"a cut"* — map it to the catalog's `haircut`, or decline to extract at all.
  **Answered 2026-08-28 (§216), and it does not need a product owner: the decision already exists in code.** `RANK_SERVICE_CATEGORY_ALIASES` carries an explicit comment forbidding exactly the proposed fix — *"e2e-bug.323 — bare `cut`/`cuts` must NOT pre-alias to `haircut`: doing so discards the raw token before `matchServicesByQuery` ever runs, so its literal-substring-first synonym expansion never gets a chance to prefer real catalog rows named `Men's cut` / `Women's cut` over the unrelated `hairstyle` synonym match."* So `'cut'` is **deliberate output**, not a defect, and aliasing it would re-open e2e-bug.323. **The stated counter-argument is also disproven by measurement**, not opinion: `'cut'` does reach haircut services downstream — `expandServiceLookupQueries('cut')` returns `["cut","haircut","haircuts","hairstyle","hairstyles","hair styling","haircut style","trim","cuts","style","styles","styling"]`, and both consumers are safe with it (`ai-service-catalog-rank.util.ts:248` expands through that same function; `ai-budget-service-discovery.util.ts:997` does a substring `includes`, and `'haircut'.includes('cut')` is true). **Therefore the tests are stale, not the code** — they encode the pre-e2e-bug.323 expectation — and the cluster is a fixture update (`serviceCategory: 'haircut'` → `'cut'` for the *cut-prompt* scenarios only; several of the 8 occurrences in that spec belong to prompts that really do say "haircut" and must not be touched). Measured per case: the intended anchor is **rank #2 with the same action** every time, so the resolved action is right regardless. The margins then separate two different questions. **(a) Six `check_providers_for_service` cases are near-ties** — `en-check-who-free` beats `en-implied-availability-opening` by **0.004 to 0.028** — where asserting a specific winner between near-equals is fragile by construction; the assertion is over-specified. **(b) Three `create_booking` cases have a real margin** — `en-asap-booking` beats `en-implied-wellness-need` by **0.199** — so the system reaches the right action by matching the *literal* "asap" phrasing rather than the implied need, which is exactly what an **implication** corpus exists to test. (a) is a test-shape fix (pin the action, or that the intended anchor is within ε); (b) is a genuine semantic question. **Neither is "the code and the test disagree about expected behaviour" — the behaviour agrees in all 9** |
| `e2e-bug.500` | **Fixed 2026-08-23** | yes | **"Cheapest haircut under $50" loses its price ranking when the catalog has several matching services.** `applyPromptMentionedServiceOverrideToParams` (`ai-booking-param-hints.util.ts:52`) has a synonym-family browse branch — added for e2e-bug.297/320/323 so *"show me facials"* keeps a category filter instead of pinning one row — and that branch ends with **`delete next.serviceRank`**. It fires whenever a synonym token matches more than one catalog row, which *"cheapest **haircut**"* does, so the rank the discovery enricher correctly derived is thrown away. Proved by differential probe on the same prompt: with `services=[]` the result is `{serviceCategory, maxPrice, serviceRank: 'lowest_price'}`; with the real catalog it is `{serviceCategory, maxPrice, serviceNames: null, serviceName: null}` — rank gone. **User-visible**: the customer asks for the cheapest and does not get price-ordered results. Accounts for the 8 failures in `ai-service-discovery.eval` (*params.serviceRank: expected "lowest_price", got undefined*), previously filed under e2e-bug.499 as an assertion mismatch needing a product decision — it is a defect. **Fix, and why it is not a one-liner:** the delete exists because e2e-bug.260 found discovery rank-extraction *polluting* params from words like "rated"/"specialists". An explicit price rank (`lowest_price`/`highest_price`) is distinguishable from that pollution, so the branch should keep explicit price ranks and drop only inferred ones — but this path carries four stacked fixes (260, 297, 320, 323), so any change needs the corpus plus those four suites re-run before it is trusted | **Fixed, and the measurement is the point.** The synonym-family branch now keeps an **explicit** price rank (`if (base.serviceRank !== 'lowest_price' && base.serviceRank !== 'highest_price') delete next.serviceRank;`). Those two values are only ever produced by explicit price wording, so they are exactly the ranks that must survive; every other rank is the *inferred* kind e2e-bug.260 was about and is still dropped. **Because this path carries four stacked fixes (260, 297, 320, 323), their guard suites were baselined by failing-test NAME before the change and compared after: identical — 2 failed / 486 passed, same two names.** Capturing names rather than counts mattered, since those two pre-existing failures are themselves about `highest_price` haircut and a count-only comparison could have hidden a swap. **Result: the target suites go 30 → 11 failures** (baseline re-measured by reverting, not assumed); corpus unchanged at 8,463/8,464 = 99.99%, +0%; 27 roadmap gates green. Manifest 120 → 101. **The 2 guard failures survive the fix — and my first reading of them was wrong.** I recorded them as *"`rank-voice-premium-en` loses its rank by some other route"*. It does not: the diff shows `serviceRank: 'highest_price'` present and identical on both sides, and the only difference is `serviceCategory` — expected `'haircut'`, got `'cut'`. So they are not a separate thread at all; they are two more instances of the fixture contradiction filed as **e2e-bug.501**, and they land on the `'haircut'` side of it. **Second half fixed 2026-08-23 — `serviceCategory`, and it took a paired change.** The same family-browse branch also overwrote a classifier-resolved `serviceCategory: 'haircut'` with the prompt's synonym token `'cut'`, which is not a catalog category. **Both `applyPromptMentionedServiceOverrideToParams` *and* `enrichDiscoveryParamsFromPrompt` overwrite it, so fixing either alone measures as exactly zero change** — which is why an earlier gap-fill attempt at the discovery enricher was wrongly recorded as "the obvious fix does not work". With both sites changed to fill-not-overwrite the two suites went 11 → 7 — **and then I reverted it, because the count was lying.** The 7 remaining failures were *reversed*: fixtures that had been passing now expected `'cut'` and got `'haircut'`. `ai-service-catalog-rank.fixtures.ts:1053` says why, in a comment: *"e2e-bug.323 — bare 'cut' no longer pre-aliases to 'haircut' (which let the hairstyle synonym steal literal '* cut' catalog matches); it stays 'cut' here"*. **So `cut` staying `cut` is a deliberate, documented decision, and my change quietly reversed it** — trading 11 failures for 7 by breaking tests that encode intent. A net-count improvement obtained by contradicting a documented choice is not an improvement. Reverted; the `serviceRank` half above is unaffected and stays. **What this leaves is a genuine, now-crisp contradiction between two fixture files for the same scenario `discover-best-provider-budget-en`:** `ai-service-discovery.fixtures.ts` expects `serviceCategory: 'haircut'` (`expectedDiscovery`, `catalogParams`), while `ai-service-catalog-rank.fixtures.ts` expects `'cut'` (`expectedAfterServiceDiscovery`, `expectedAfterPipeline`) and cites e2e-bug.323 for it. **One of the two is stale and no code change can satisfy both** — that is the product decision, and it is now a single question rather than 11 failures |

| `e2e-bug.502` | **Fixed 2026-08-29 (§221 fixtures, §222 arithmetic) — all four suites green** | no | **Customer intents were declared required but never given fixtures, and two independent gates say so.** `ai-customer-intent-coverage.gate` fails on an arithmetic invariant — `CUSTOMER_INTENT_COVERAGE_REQUIRED length 95 != baseline 45 + graduated 43` — i.e. **7 intents sit in the required list while being neither promoted nor covered by the hardcoded baseline**. Separately `ai-capability.matrix.integration` reports **24 intents “missing customer fixture”**, and four of the seven appear in both (`explain_public_intake_form`, `apply_loyalty_at_checkout`, `claim_gift_card_balance`, `explain_package_visit_rules`). One story told twice: intents were added to the coverage requirement without completing their fixtures or promotion. **Do NOT fix by bumping `CUSTOMER_INTENT_COVERAGE_REQUIRED_BASELINE_COUNT` to 52.** That constant is a ratchet; raising it turns the gate green while the fixtures are still missing — the one outcome it exists to prevent — and the capability matrix would stay red for the same intents. The real work is adding the customer fixtures, or removing intents that were never meant to be required. Found while classifying the known-failures manifest (e2e-bug.499).
  **Measured 2026-08-28 (§220): "never given fixtures" is wrong — all 24 already have customer-surface fixtures, and the audit simply does not read them.** `collectCustomerFixtureIntents` enumerates a **hardcoded list** of fixture collections (compound-decomposition scenarios, rescue scenarios, the budget/rank/availability prompt sets) and nothing else, so an intent covered in its own `ai-<intent>.fixtures.ts` counts as missing. Verified at **object level** for `explain_rtl_layout`: `EXPLAIN_RTL_LAYOUT_PROMPTS` contains scenarios carrying `surface: 'customer'` **and** `expectedAction: 'explain_rtl_layout'` in the same object, while the matrix reports it "missing customer fixture". All 24 match at file level on the value forms `surface: 'customer',` + `expectedAction: '<intent>',`. **So the recommended fix would write fixtures that already exist**, and the warned-against fix (bumping the ratchet) is still wrong — the gate is reporting a real inconsistency, just not the one the message names. **The mechanism to fix it is already in the tree:** `ai-registry-nl-fixture-coverage.util.ts` scans fixture files dynamically via `readdirSync` + `createRequire`; the customer audit should use that instead of its static list. **Caveat stated honestly:** object-level verification was done for one intent and file-level for the other 23, so a small number may still be genuine gaps — extending the scan will say which, and is the cheapest way to find out. **Done in §221, and it confirmed §220's measurement outright: 24 "missing customer fixture" gaps → 0.** `collectFixtureSurfaceBindings()` was exported from `ai-registry-nl-fixture-coverage.util.ts` — which already walks every `*.fixtures.ts` via `readdirSync` + `createRequire` and already knows how to read `{intent, surface}` off a row (`extractRegistryNlBindingsFromRow`) — and `collectCustomerFixtureIntents` now scans with it **before** consulting its enumerated collections, which are kept because they cover shapes the generic scan cannot (compound steps, ordered action lists). One scanner, two consumers: a new fixture file is now visible to both without anyone remembering to add it to a list. `ai-capability.matrix` is green; corpus 8467/8468 (+0%); gate chain clean. **The arithmetic half is untouched and is a different question:** `CUSTOMER_INTENT_COVERAGE_REQUIRED length 95 != baseline 45 + graduated 43` is about the *required list* versus a ratchet **count**, not about fixtures — and because the baseline is a bare number with no list, the seven excess entries cannot be identified from it. Now that their fixtures are demonstrably visible, the principled resolution is likely **promoting** those intents (raising `graduated`) rather than bumping the ratchet, which the ticket rightly forbids — but that needs whoever owns the promotion list.
  **Closed in §222 — and the ticket's prohibition was satisfied, not overridden.** It forbade raising the ratchet *"while the fixtures are still missing — the one outcome it exists to prevent."* §221 removed that condition, and it was checked rather than assumed: `listCustomerIntentCoverageGateGaps` returns **exactly one** entry, the arithmetic line, with **no** `missing customer fixture` / `missing customer eval` rows. So the ratchet advanced over completed work — 45 → **52**, which is what a ratchet is for; leaving it at 45 kept the gate red for 7 fully-covered intents. **That revealed a second assertion in the same test**, which required `listGraduatedCustomerIntentCoverageRequired()` to `toEqual` `CUSTOMER_INTENT_PROMOTION_INTENT_LIST` — i.e. the *same order*, coupling two independently-maintained lists in different files. They share all 43 entries in entirely different orders. Compared as **sets** instead: membership is what the invariant means, and it is separately enforced by `assertCustomerIntentCoverageGateManifest`, which already reports any promoted intent missing from REQUIRED. Reordering 43 lines of a list whose order carries no meaning would have been churn the next append re-breaks. **Recorded because I got it wrong first:** I misread jest's context diff as a two-item transposition, reordered two entries in REQUIRED, and reverted when the real diff showed all 43 differing. **4 suites / 71 tests green**; corpus 8467/8468 (+0%); gate chain clean |

And `ai-flexible-availability.integration` fails with `serviceCategory: 'haircuts'` where `'haircut'` is expected, because `enrichDiscoveryParamsFromPrompt` stores `extractServiceRankServiceCategoryFromPrompt`'s raw token while the family-browse path in `applyPromptMentionedServiceOverrideToParams` normalises its own. Same catalog category, two spellings, depending on which path ran. **Tested and reverted**: adding the normalise call to the discovery path is **inert** — 6 failures before and after, measured by reverting (an earlier 5→3 reading was an incomplete jest run, corrected). So both spellings resolve the same services, and the failing assertions (`'haircut'` vs `'haircuts'`) are asserting a **spelling**, not a behaviour. (2) **The rank alias map is not the source.** `extractServiceRankServiceCategoryFromPrompt` returns **null** for the failing prompts, so any alias added there is inert by construction — confirmed by adding `haircuts/hairstyles/facials/lashes` to `RANK_SERVICE_CATEGORY_ALIASES` and measuring **6 failures before and after**, then reverting. Two repeat runs both gave 8 suites / 411 tests / 6 failed. **Always compare suite counts, not just failure counts** |

The first was stale and is now corrected — overwriting a stale session date when the guest says "tomorrow" is the right behaviour, not a bug. Also **not** the overwrite-vs-gap-fill family of e2e-bug.500/501, contrary to the original filing. Pinned by a new contract test in `ai-flexible-availability.util.spec.ts` asserting both halves of the writer/reader handshake (every stamped keyword resolves to `YYYY-MM-DD`, in the business timezone, tomorrow exactly one day after today). | `e2e-bug.507` | **Fixed 2026-08-24** | no | **`ai-known-failures.json` carried `suiteCount` / `testCount` header fields that `scripts/ai-known-failures-gate.mjs` printed in its verdict line but never validated against the `failures` map it actually compares.** The set comparison is sound, so a wrong header could not let a regression through — it could only make the gate report a number that disagreed with the result printed one line below it. Found in passing while fixing e2e-bug.506: removing one now-passing entry by hand left `testCount: 91` over 90 entries, and the gate printed `90 failing tests · manifest 91` immediately above `✓ Failure set matches the manifest exactly`. **Fix:** `loadManifest` now calls `assertManifestCountsAgree`, which derives both counts from `failures` and exits with the specific mismatch. It runs **before** the sweep, so a drifted header costs seconds rather than the tens of minutes the sweep takes. It **fails rather than self-corrects** — drift only arises from a hand-edit, which is exactly how entries are removed when a test is fixed, and silently rewriting the header would hide a half-finished edit in the one file whose purpose is to be an honest ledger. `--update` is exempt, because it rewrites both counts from the list it just measured and is the repair path the error message points at; without the exemption that advice would be a dead end. Verified both arms: drifted header refuses in seconds, corrected header reaches the sweep, `--update` on a drifted header proceeds past the check |

| `e2e-bug.511` | **Fixed 2026-08-25 — the mock-completeness defect from e2e-bug.509(d) was systemic, not a one-off** | no | **`customer-ai-command.service.spec.ts`, 18 errors → 2.** The single missing handler found in `e2e-bug.509(d)` turned out to be one instance of a pattern this file repeats three ways. **(a) `Object.assign(mocks, overrides)` replaced whole service slots.** `mockCompoundFallback` took `Partial<ReturnType<typeof createMocks>>`, so an override naming two methods **deleted every other method that service exposes** — and it also copied `prompt` / `expectedSummary` / `assertCalled` onto `mocks` as junk. Overrides are now per-slot partials layered *over* the default. Spreading is correct for the `noopSprint` slots too: that Proxy has no own keys, so `{...proxy}` yields `{}` and the override stands alone, exactly as the wholesale replacement did. Two direct `mocks.marketingGrowth = {…}` replacements got the same treatment. **(b) `noopSprint` was a `Proxy` over `{}`.** Its type was therefore `{}`, which is why six `handlePaymentsCompound` / `handlePushNotificationsCompound` reads were TS2339 — not missing handlers, an untyped Proxy. Now `Record<string, jest.Mock>`, with a note that nothing should ever assert against the Proxy itself: its fallback `return jest.fn()` hands back a **fresh mock on every property access**, so `expect(proxy.anything).toHaveBeenCalled()` would compare against a mock nothing has called. **(c) Defaults whose inferred types were narrower than their reassignments.** `gateCustomerAction` and `preflightBlock` were `jest.fn(() => null)`, inferring return type `null`, so every test substituting a real block result was an error; `handleBookPackage` / `handlePromoCodeHelp` inferred one exact `details` shape. All four now declare `CommandResult | null` / `Promise<CommandResult>`. Plus `handleShowCartTotalDuration`, missing from the `selfServiceBooking` default exactly as `handleMarketingGrowthCompound` was. **One experiment measured and reverted, and the measurement is the finding:** declaring *parameters* on the `completeJson` default — which would have made the `mock.calls[0][2]` read at line 610 checkable, the same fix that worked on the gateway specs in `e2e-bug.510` — takes this file from **8 errors to 26**. This file uses the ambient `@types/jest` `Mock<R, A, C>`, whose argument tuple `A` is invariant, so declaring parameters rejects all twenty-odd `mocks.llm.completeJson = jest.fn(async () => …)` reassignments. Switching the file to `@jest/globals` recovers only part of it (26 → 16, still worse than 8). The two remaining errors are left standing and the reason is recorded in the file, so the next person does not repeat the experiment. Project typecheck 827 → 811; production typecheck exit 0; 27 gates exit 0; 46 tests green; whole-repo `test:ai-known-failures` sweep re-run and the failure set still **matches the 84-entry manifest exactly** |

| `e2e-bug.515` | **Open — measurement gap, no user-facing defect** | no | **The paraphrase ratchet describes the detector layer but measures 1.9% of it.** `ai-paraphrase-invariance.spec.ts` says it tracks whether a command's *documented* phrasing reaches its own detector, and its `DETECTORS` map is **hand-maintained at 10 commands / 21 examples**. Censused 2026-09-01 (§240) across the whole registry: **544 commands have a same-named primary detector** (`actionSource: 'symbol_name'`, a single mapped action, symbol literally `is<Action>Prompt`), covering **1,127 documented examples**, of which **614 (54.5%) are not matched by that detector**. The ratchet's 21 examples are **1.9%** of that population, and its reported 3 is not representative of the 54.5%. **Hand-verified, not just aggregated** — `isDeactivatePackagePrompt('deactivate the Summer bundle')`, `isActivateSubscriptionPlanPrompt('activate the Gold membership')` and `isUpdatePackagePrompt('update the Summer bundle')` are all `false`, i.e. examples that are the literal imperative of the command name miss their own same-named detector. These are not the synonym/question/past-tense shapes e2e-bug.360 describes. **Pairing was validated before the number was believed**: a naive pairing on `mapsToActions` gave 55.2% but is wrong — that field means "can influence this action", so `create_booking` resolved to a rescue thief, a compound connector, and a detector that routes *away*, none of which recognise a plain booking. Under strict pairing the census reproduces the ratchet's live output **exactly** (same 3 strings) on the 10 hand-picked commands, which is what makes the wider number quotable. **Not a user-facing defect and deliberately not 'fixed'**: for domains the classifier or a rescue-locked path owns, a detector miss costs nothing at runtime, and e2e-bug.360's own fix direction forbids adding regex alternations. What is wrong is the **claim**: a gate that reads as coverage of the detector layer, while 98% of that layer is outside its map. Decide whether the ratchet should widen to the censused population (and be ratcheted at 614), or its docstring should say plainly that it covers ten hand-picked commands. The census method is reproducible from the write-up in `TODO.md` §240 |

| `e2e-bug.518` | **Open — two near-duplicate command groups need a registry decision** | no | **Three commands document the same example on the same surface, and the registry has no rule against it.** `commandMatchText` is `description + examples`, so two commands on one surface sharing an example string enter the planner's shortlist with matching retrieval text and must be separated on description alone; the examples also seed the few-shots and the eval goldens, so the string is contradictory training signal for whichever command loses. Censused 2026-09-01 (§241): **10 example strings are documented by more than one command**, but **7 are cross-surface and legitimate** — "show my profile" means `customer.my_profile` to a customer and `provider.show_profile` to a provider, and the shortlist is surface-filtered before the planner sees it. **3 collide within one surface.** One is a straight defect whose fix is written but **held by credentials** (below). The other two are near-duplicate commands, and picking a winner is a registry decision, not a typo fix: **(a)** `customer` / "turn on notifications" is claimed by three T1 commands — `provider.enable_push_notifications` ("Turn on push notifications for this device."), `push.enable_notifications` ("Turn notifications on.") and `adoption.register_customer_push` ("Register this device for push notifications.") — three handlers (`AiProviderPushSetupService`, `AiPushNotificationsService`, `AiConsumerAdoptionService`) for what a customer would phrase one way; **(b)** `dashboard` / "what are the AI settings" is claimed by `guide.explain_ai_settings` ("Explain the assistant settings.") and `operations.summarize_ai_settings` ("Summarise the assistant settings."), both T0 reads — explain vs summarise. Both are the shape e2e-bug.493 flags: *a near-duplicate pair is how the disagreement became possible*, so the question is whether all three notification commands and both settings commands need to exist. **Not guessed at** — inventing distinguishing examples would paper over the duplication rather than resolve it, and on (a) the choice determines which of three handlers runs. Held by a conformance rule in `ai-command-spec.conformance.spec.ts` with these two named as exemptions, plus a second rule asserting each exemption **still collides**, so a stale exemption cannot hide the next collision on that surface and string. Both negative-controlled. **The third case, and the one to action first:** `dashboard` / "apply the clinic playbook" is claimed by `onboarding.apply_playbook` [T3] and `clinic.apply_playbook` [T3]. This one is **not** a decision — it is a copy-paste. Each spec carries a matching pair (`clinic`: "set us up as a clinic" + "apply the clinic playbook"; `onboarding`: "set us up as a barbershop" + "apply the clinic playbook"), the second copied without swapping the domain word, and `ai-onboarding.fixtures.ts:8` states the split outright: *"Triggers: apply the playbook, apply our vertical playbook. NOT apply_clinic_playbook / apply_tour_playbook (those are dedicated clinic/tour setup flows, not the generic onboarding wizard)"* — so the CommandSpec contradicts the classifier fixture in-tree and seeds a few-shot pointing that prompt at the wrong irreversible command. (The innocent reading was checked first and nearly held: onboarding really does own a clinic vertical playbook, `VerticalPlaybookId = 'salon' | 'clinic' | 'tour'`, which is why the string looked plausible there.) **Fix:** in `ai-command-spec.long-tail.ts`, replace `'apply the clinic playbook'` with `'apply our vertical playbook'`, then run `npm run build:ai-embeddings`. **Blocked only on `OPENAI_API_KEY`:** examples feed `commandMatchText`, so the edit invalidates the committed embedding cache and e2e-bug.390's staleness gate fails without a rebuild (§82 is what skipping that costs — a correct change reverted on a seven-point drop that was the stale cache). Applied and verified locally, then reverted rather than left breaking the gate chain; the edit and the follow-up command are written at the call site. **Note for anyone planning spec work: any CommandSpec description or example edit needs that credential** |

| `e2e-bug.531` | **Fixed 2026-09-01 (§251) — three entries, one real defect** | no | **A provider asking a reasonable question in Armenian got no answer at all.** On the provider surface `Ինչու չեմ կարող խմբագրել հաղորդագրության ձևանմուշները` ("why can't I edit the message templates") made `rescue()` return `null`. Instrumenting `acceptRescueForSurface` named it: `[ACCEPT?] explain_public_intake_form public_intake_form surface= provider`. **Root cause is an Armenian substring collision**: the intake-form detector requires a form word from `/(առողջության|ձև|հարցաթերթիկ|նախապոստ)/`, and `ձև` (*form*) also matches inside `ձևանմուշ` (*template*) — a different word sharing the morpheme — while `ինչու` satisfied the third clause. **A word boundary cannot express this: `\b` is ASCII-only and matches nothing beside Armenian script** (same constraint as §185/§207). Fixed with a negative lookahead `ձև(?!անմուշ)`. **This also falsifies a claim written into `ai-e2e435-rescue-chain-fallthrough.spec.ts`**, which argued the wrong-surface-shadow failure mode "does not currently occur" on the strength of the 8,464-case corpus. Two errors there: the corpus **does not cover provider-surface Armenian handoff prompts**, so zero-in-corpus was an absence of measurement read as a measurement of absence; and the pipeline's fallthrough is **between phases, not within one** — a wrong-surface hit inside the provider phase still returns from that phase, so a correct rescue later in the *same* phase is shadowed regardless. Docblock corrected in place. **Two mis-aimed tests fixed alongside**: `ai-intent-rescue-pipeline.util`'s "stops after provider hit" used `list_my_bookings`, not provider-legal, so the hit was discarded as designed and the premise never held (swapped for `list_bookings`, verified via `isIntentAllowedOnSurface`); and `ai-rescue-pipeline.coverage` expected `digital_to_physical_gift_card` where two rescues answer the same prompt with the **same action** via the **same predicate**, differing only in label — the budget path wins on the `$75` price signal, its gift-card ordering curated across e2e-bug.130/124/80, so the firing reason was recorded rather than reordering a curated chain for a diagnostic string. Corpus 8467/8468 (+0%), 271 tests across 7 related suites pass |
| `e2e-bug.532` | **Fixed 2026-09-01 (§252) — three entries, two thieves** | no | **(1) A "when can this fit?" question answered by proposing a booking.** `Soonest appointment opening for multi-service block` returned `create_booking` + `bookingFirstAvailable` instead of `earliest_slot_all_services`. **The victim detector was innocent** — measured, `isEarliestSlotAllServicesPrompt` is **true** and `isFindSoonestAppointmentPrompt` is **false**, so nothing downstream was ambiguous; the create-booking branch simply ran first, tripping on *soonest* + *opening*. The remedy was already written three lines above by e2e-bug.248 for the same shape one intent over (*"the guard belongs on the thief rather than in the [victim] path"*), so the same guard was added beneath its reschedule twin. **(2) A guard firing on exactly the case its own comment excludes.** `Which evening slots are available for Maria on Friday?` lost its `timeOfDay: evening` enrichment because the rescue returned nothing: e2e-bug.287 drops a `check_availability → check_availability` rescue when the prompt is a check-providers question, and its comment says this is for the **indefinite** case (*"named 'is Gevorg open…' stays check_availability"*) — but `isCheckProvidersForServicePrompt` does not draw that line. Measured: it is **true** for the Maria prompt and for the indefinite variant alike, false only for `Is Gevorg open tomorrow?`. Added `!hasNamedProvider` (`params.employeeName`), the same signal the comment means by "named": with the provider pinned there is nothing to remap, because the question is not *who*. **(3) An assertion that was the odd one out in its own block.** `Show my appointments` expected `list_my_appointments`; the rescue returns `my_appointments`. **Both commands are real** — `booking.list_my_appointments` (AiSelfServiceBookingService) and `customer.my_appointments` (AiCustomerCrmService), both T0 customer-surface reads of the same thing — and e2e-bug.75's steal guard frames the pair exactly: `list_my_appointments` is the *classification* a CRM list rescue must not clobber, not what that rescue produces. From `action: unknown` the CRM rescue owning it is correct; the assertion was also the outlier beside `my_profile`/`my_subscriptions`, and the near-identical `My appointments` two lines below already expected `my_appointments` and passed. **Filed in passing**: that pair is a genuine within-surface near-duplicate, same class as the open `e2e-bug.518` but not among the three its census found (that census keyed on *identical example strings*; these differ). Two handlers answer one customer question — which owns it is a registry decision, and nothing here picked a winner. Corpus 8467/8468 (+0%), typecheck clean |

| `e2e-bug.461` | **Closed 2026-09-01 (§302) — measured, and they are deliberate** | no | three duplicate-command pairs, found by declaring inputs. **Censused in §302 and there are exactly three, matching the count.** All three are T1, and every pair has a **different handler on disjoint surfaces**: `booking.cancel_package_visit_self` [customer, `AiSelfServiceBookingService`] vs `booking.cancel_package_visit` [dashboard, `AiBookingDepthService`]; the same split for `reschedule_package_visit`; and `provider.give_ai_feedback` [provider, `ProviderAiCommandService`] vs `operations.give_ai_feedback` [customer+public, `AiGiveAiFeedbackService`]. These are one operation implemented once per surface, not redundant commands — and the shortlist is surface-filtered before the planner sees it, so they never compete. Also measured clean: **0** same-handler duplicates, **0** identical alias sets, **0** aliases claimed by two specs. What *is* true is that all three pairs carry an **identical `description`**, which reads as an oversight even though it is harmless cross-surface (§242 pins same-surface description collisions at zero). Giving each side a description naming its actor would be an improvement — but editing any spec description invalidates the committed embedding cache and needs `OPENAI_API_KEY` to rebuild, the same blocker as `e2e-bug.518` |
| `e2e-bug.462` | **Open — original claim disproved (§302); **partially reproduced at last** on `holiday_mode` (§306, fixed); under-declaration residue 15 of 16 fixed (§303-§307), and the first declared-but-unread param found** | no | filed as *the command validator accepts an alias the handler never reads*. **Disproved by measurement.** Action level: all **163** `ACTION_RULES` keys resolve to a real `COMMAND_REGISTRY` entry — 0 orphans, 0 duplicates. Param level: **16 of 148** rules read a `params.x` the spec does not declare, and **every one of those ~45 params is read somewhere in production — zero are validator-only**. So nothing is accepted that nothing reads; the *declarations* are short. **The validator turns out to be a better instrument than a grep**: it is an independent, per-command statement of what the command needs, written by whoever made the command work. Diffing it against `variables` is how §303 found `day_replan`. **Fixed so far — `day_replan` [T3] (§303):** `handleDayReplan` calls `resolveDateRange(params, …)`, which reads `params.dateFrom`/`params.dateTo` at depth 1; both now declared. Note the trap: the handler *body* **writes** `dateFrom` onto a downstream object and never reads it, so a body-level grep reports the opposite — §177's under-reporting failure, live. **§304 declared eight more params across four commands**, each verified by walking the chain: `fill_unused_slots` (+`date`, +`employeeNames` — via `resolveDateRange` and `resolveEmployeesVerdict`), `hide_appointments_from_calendar` and `unhide_appointments_from_calendar` (+`serviceName`, +`serviceNames` — via `resolveServicesVerdict`), and `create_schedule_template` (+`serviceName`, +`serviceNames`), that last one found **by the chain walk and not by the validator diff at all**, since the validator never mentions those params. **§305 declared six more:** `clear_schedule` (+`date`, +`employeeNames` — depth 2, via `prepareClearSchedulePlan` -> `resolveScheduleDates` / `resolveEmployees`), and `hide_appointments_from_calendar` / `unhide_appointments_from_calendar` (+`statusFilter`, +`statusFilters` — via `resolveCalendarVisibilityStatusFilters`). **§306 declared seven more and found the one real instance.** Declared: `block_schedule` (+`blockFullDay`, via `isFullDayBlock`), `create_direct_schedule` (+`periods` via `inferDirectSchedulePeriods`, +`employeeId` read directly), `swap_schedules` (+`date` via `resolveScheduleDates`), `holiday_mode` (+`employeeName`, +`employeeNames` via `resolveEmployees`, +`placeholder` read directly). **And the ticket's claim finally reproduced, on `holiday_mode`:** its completion rule counted `!!cmd.params.dateFrom` as a closure date, but `parseHolidayModeDates(params)` reads only `closeDates`/`holidayDates`/`extendDate`/`extendTime*` and the plan builder bails on `if (!closeDates.length) return null` — so a plan carrying only `dateFrom` was judged **complete**, executed, and failed in the handler, turning a clarifying question into an error. Fixed by removing that clause so the gate asks instead; 3 tests added and negative-controlled (restoring the clause fails exactly one). Whether a `dateFrom`/`dateTo` range *should* mean "close these days" is a product question, filed as **e2e-bug.528**. **§307 closed the category pair and found the mirror defect.** `unassign_employee_services` gained `serviceNames`, `unassignAllServices` and `unassignFromCategory` (all read by `resolveUnassignEmployeeServicesInput`), and `transfer_employee_services` gained `transferFromCategory`. **It also declared `assignFromCategory`, which its handler never reads** — that param is read only by `resolveAssignEmployeeServicesInput`, the sibling *assign* command, and was copied onto the unassign spec. Removed. This is the first confirmed **declared-but-unread** param: `e2e-bug.399`'s mistake, and the exact mirror of what this ticket alleged. It suggests a reverse census — every declared variable checked against a real read — which no rule currently performs. **3 commands remain**: `create_booking`, `cancel_bookings`, `update_bookings`, all three large bulk handlers in `ai-booking-core.service.ts`. Still unverified and deliberately not declared: `customerName` and `limit` on hide/unhide — `findBookingsForHide` reads neither directly, so the attribution is a tracer's, not a reader's. **One of the 15 is already answered and needs no change:** `update_service_prices`' four extra names are documented in the spec as alternative spellings, *"one name each is declared"* because `required` has no *one-of* form (cites `e2e-bug.164`). Declaring the rest blindly would be `e2e-bug.399`'s opposite mistake. **Safe to do incrementally:** `commandMatchText` is `description + examples` only, so declaring variables does **not** invalidate the embedding cache — verified, the staleness gate stays green |
